      <h1 class="fgh">SCHOOL BREAKFAST AND LUNCH</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.111">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.111 SCHOOL MEALS POLICIES; LUNCH AID; FOOD SERVICE ACCOUNTING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.111.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">School meals policies.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Each Minnesota participant in the national school lunch program must adopt and post to its website, or the website of the organization where the meal is served, a school meals policy.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The policy must be in writing and clearly communicate student meal charges when payment cannot be collected at the point of service. The policy must be reasonable and well-defined and maintain the dignity of students by prohibiting lunch shaming or otherwise ostracizing the student.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The policy must address whether the participant uses a collections agency to collect unpaid school meals debt.</p>
          <p>(d) The policy must ensure that once a participant has placed a meal on a tray or otherwise served the meal to a student, the meal may not be subsequently withdrawn from the student by the cashier or other school official, whether or not the student has an outstanding meals balance.</p>
          <p>(e) The policy must ensure that a student who has been determined eligible for free and reduced-price meals must always be served a reimbursable meal even if the student has an outstanding debt.</p>
          <p>(f) If a school contracts with a third party for its meal services, it must provide the vendor with its school meals policy. Any contract between the school and a third-party provider entered into or modified after July 1, 2021, must ensure that the third-party provider adheres to the participant's school meals policy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.111.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">School lunch aid amounts.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Each school year, the state must pay participants in the national school lunch program either:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the amount of 12.5 cents for each full paid and free student lunch and 52.5 cents for each reduced-price lunch served to students; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) if the school participates in the free school meals program under subdivision 1c, the sum of: (i) 12.5 cents for each full paid, reduced-price, and free student lunch; and (ii) the amount specified in subdivision 1d.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.111.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district, charter school, nonpublic school, or other participant in the national school lunch program must apply to the department for school meals payments in the manner provided by the department.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.111.1c">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1c.<span class="headnote">Free school meals program.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The free school meals program is created within the Department of Education.</p>
          <p>(b) Each school that participates in the United States Department of Agriculture National School Lunch program and has an Identified Student Percentage below the federal percentage determined for all meals to be reimbursed at the free rate via the Community Eligibility Provision must participate in the free school meals program.</p>
          <p>(c) Each school that participates in the United States Department of Agriculture National School Lunch program and has an Identified Student Percentage at or above the federal percentage determined for all meals to be reimbursed at the free rate must participate in the federal Community Eligibility Provision in order to participate in the free school meals program.</p>
          <p>(d) Each school that participates in the free school meals program must:</p>
          <p>(1) participate in the United States Department of Agriculture School Breakfast Program and the United States Department of Agriculture National School Lunch Program; and</p>
          <p>(2) provide to all students at no cost up to two federally reimbursable meals per school day, with a maximum of one free breakfast and one free lunch.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.111.1d">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1d.<span class="headnote">Free school meals program aid amount.</span></h2>
          <p>The department must provide to every Minnesota school participating in the free school meals program state funding for each school lunch and breakfast served to a student, with a maximum of one breakfast and one lunch per student per school day. The state aid equals the difference between the applicable federal reimbursement rate at that school site for a free meal, as determined annually by the United States Department of Agriculture, and the actual federal reimbursement received by the participating school for the breakfast or lunch served to the student.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.111.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/18/#laws.0.1.0">2023 c 18 s 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.111.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Federal child and adult care food program and federal summer food service program; criteria and notice.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must post on the department's website eligibility criteria and application information for nonprofit organizations interested in applying to the commissioner for approval as a multisite sponsoring organization under the federal child and adult care food program and federal summer food service program. The posted criteria and information must inform interested nonprofit organizations about:</p>
          <p>(1) the criteria the commissioner uses to approve or disapprove an application, including how an applicant demonstrates financial viability for the Minnesota program, among other criteria;</p>
          <p>(2) the commissioner's process and time line for notifying an applicant when its application is approved or disapproved and, if the application is disapproved, the explanation the commissioner provides to the applicant; and</p>
          <p>(3) any appeal or other recourse available to a disapproved applicant.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must evaluate financial eligibility as part of the application process. An organization applying to be a prospective nonprofit multisite sponsoring organization for the federal child and adult care food program or the federal summer food service program must provide documentation of financial viability as an organization. Documentation must include:</p>
          <p>(1) evidence that the organization has operated for at least one year and has filed at least one tax return;</p>
          <p>(2) the most recent tax return submitted by the organization and corresponding forms and financial statements;</p>
          <p>(3) a profit and loss statement and balance sheet or similar financial information; and</p>
          <p>(4) evidence that at least ten percent of the organization's operating revenue comes from sources other than the United States Department of Agriculture child nutrition program and that the organization has additional funds or a performance bond available to cover at least one month of reimbursement claims.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.111.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">School food service fund.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The expenses described in this subdivision must be recorded as provided in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) In each district, the expenses for a school food service program for pupils must be attributed to a school food service fund. Under a food service program, the school food service may prepare or serve milk, meals, or snacks in connection with school or community service activities.</p>
          <p>(c) Revenues and expenditures for food service activities must be recorded in the food service fund. The costs of processing applications, accounting for meals, preparing and serving food, providing kitchen custodial services, and other expenses involving the preparing of meals or the kitchen section of the lunchroom may be charged to the food service fund or to the general fund of the district. The costs of lunchroom supervision, lunchroom custodial services, lunchroom utilities, lunchroom furniture, and other administrative costs of the food service program must be charged to the general fund.</p>
          <p>That portion of superintendent and fiscal manager costs that can be documented as attributable to the food service program may be charged to the food service fund provided that the school district does not employ or contract with a food service director or other individual who manages the food service program, or food service management company. If the cost of the superintendent or fiscal manager is charged to the food service fund, the charge must be at a wage rate not to exceed the statewide average for food service directors as determined by the department.</p>
          <p>(d) Capital expenditures for the purchase of food service equipment must be made from the general fund and not the food service fund, unless the restricted balance in the food service fund at the end of the last fiscal year is greater than the cost of the equipment to be purchased.</p>
          <p>(e) If the condition set out in paragraph (d) applies, the equipment may be purchased from the food service fund.</p>
          <p>(f) If a deficit in the food service fund exists at the end of a fiscal year, and the deficit is not eliminated by revenues from food service operations in the next fiscal year, then the deficit must be eliminated by a permanent fund transfer from the general fund at the end of that second fiscal year. However, if a district contracts with a food service management company during the period in which the deficit has accrued, the deficit must be eliminated by a payment from the food service management company.</p>
          <p>(g) Notwithstanding paragraph (f), a district may incur a deficit in the food service fund for up to three years without making the permanent transfer if the district submits to the commissioner by January 1 of the second fiscal year a plan for eliminating that deficit at the end of the third fiscal year.</p>
          <p>(h) If a surplus in the food service fund exists at the end of a fiscal year for three successive years, a district may recode for that fiscal year the costs of lunchroom supervision, lunchroom custodial services, lunchroom utilities, lunchroom furniture, and other administrative costs of the food service program charged to the general fund according to paragraph (c) and charge those costs to the food service fund in a total amount not to exceed the amount of surplus in the food service fund.</p>
          <p>(i) For purposes of this subdivision, "lunchroom furniture" means tables and chairs regularly used by pupils in a lunchroom from which they may consume milk, meals, or snacks in connection with school or community service activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.111.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">No fees.</span></h2>
          <p>A participant that receives school lunch aid under this section must make lunch available without charge and must not deny a school lunch to all participating students who qualify for free or reduced-price meals, whether or not that student has an outstanding balance in the student's meals account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.111.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Respectful treatment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The participant must also provide meals to students in a respectful manner according to the policy adopted under subdivision 1. The participant must ensure that any reminders for payment of outstanding student meal balances do not demean or stigmatize any child participating in the school lunch program, including but not limited to dumping meals; withdrawing a meal that has been served; announcing or listing students' names publicly; providing alternative meals not specifically related to dietary needs; providing nonreimbursable meals; or affixing stickers, stamps, or pins. The participant must not impose any other restriction prohibited under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.37">123B.37</a> due to unpaid student meal balances. The participant must not limit a student's participation in any school activities, graduation ceremonies, field trips, athletics, activity clubs, or other extracurricular activities or access to materials, technology, or other items provided to students due to an unpaid student meal balance.</p>
          <p>(b) If the commissioner or the commissioner's designee determines a participant has violated the requirement to provide meals to participating students in a respectful manner, the commissioner or the commissioner's designee must send a letter of noncompliance to the participant. The participant is required to respond and, if applicable, remedy the practice within 60 days.</p>
        </div>
      </div>

			">

<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.7.2.0">2013 c 116 art 7 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/125/#laws.1.27.0">2013 c 125 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.5.1.0">2014 c 272 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.19.1.0">2014 c 312 art 19 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.27.3.0">2016 c 189 art 27 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/13/#laws.8.1.0">1Sp2021 c 13 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/18/#laws.0.1.0">2023 c 18 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.9.1.0">2023 c 55 art 9 s 1</a>-3,19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.7.1.0">2024 c 109 art 7 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.9.1.0">2024 c 115 art 9 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.1115">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.1115 FREE AND REDUCED-PRICE SCHOOL MEALS PROGRAM DATA SHARING.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) Each school participating in the federal school lunch program shall electronically send to the Department of Education the eligibility information on each child who is eligible for the free and reduced-price meals program, unless the child's parent or legal guardian after being notified of the potential disclosure of this information for the limited purpose stated in paragraph (b) elects not to have the information disclosed.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) Pursuant to United States Code, title 42, section 1758(b)(6)(A), the Department of Education shall enter into an agreement with the Department of Human Services to share the eligibility information provided by each school in paragraph (a) for the limited purpose of identifying children who may be eligible for medical assistance or MinnesotaCare. The Department of Human Services must ensure that this information remains confidential and shall only be used for this purpose. Any unauthorized disclosure shall be subject to a penalty.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.1115.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/358/#laws.3.1.0">2008 c 358 art 3 s 1</a>; 2023 c 55 art 9 s 19</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.112"><b>124D.112</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.6.38.0">1998 c 398 art 6 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.7.3.0">1999 c 241 art 7 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.113"><b>124D.113</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.7.3.0">1999 c 241 art 7 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.114">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.114 LACTOSE REDUCED MILK.</h1>
        <p>(a) If a nonpublic school or district receives school lunch aid under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.111">124D.111</a> or participates in the school breakfast program and receives a written request from the parent of a pupil who is lactose intolerant, then the nonpublic school or district must make available lactose reduced milk; milk fortified with lactase in liquid, tablet, granular, or other form; or milk to which lactobacillus acidophilus has been added for the pupil.</p>
        <p>(b) Notwithstanding any law, local ordinance, or local regulation to the contrary, a school may pour or serve portions of any product required by this section from a large container of the product at the time and place the pupil is being served.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.114.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/510/#laws.0.1.0">1988 c 510 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.76.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 76</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.18.0">2000 c 254 s 18</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.115"><b>124D.115</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.5.37.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 5 s 37</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.1155"><b>124D.1155</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.5.14.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 5 s 14</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.1156"><b>124D.1156</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.5.37.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 5 s 37</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.1158">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.1158 SCHOOL BREAKFAST PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.1158.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose; eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The purpose of the school breakfast program is to provide affordable morning nutrition to children so that they can effectively learn.</p>
          <p>(b) A school district, charter school, nonpublic school, or other participant in the federal school breakfast program may receive state breakfast aid.</p>
          <p>(c) Schools shall encourage all children to eat a nutritious breakfast, either at home or at school, and shall work to eliminate barriers to breakfast participation at school such as inadequate facilities and transportation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.1158.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/18/#laws.0.2.0">2023 c 18 s 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.1158.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Program reimbursement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Each school year, the state must reimburse each participating school either:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) 30 cents for each reduced-price breakfast, 55 cents for each fully paid breakfast served to students in grades 1 to 12, and $1.30 for each fully paid breakfast served to a prekindergarten student enrolled in an approved voluntary prekindergarten program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.08">142D.08</a>, early childhood special education student participating in a program authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.08">142D.08</a>, or a kindergarten student; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) if the school participates in the free school meals program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.111#stat.124D.111.1c">124D.111, subdivision 1c</a>, state aid as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.111#stat.124D.111.1d">124D.111, subdivision 1d</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.1158.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">No fees.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A school that receives school breakfast aid under this section must make breakfast available without charge to all participating students in grades 1 to 12 who qualify for free or reduced-price meals and to all prekindergarten students enrolled in an approved voluntary prekindergarten program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.08">142D.08</a>, early childhood special education students participating in a program authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.08">142D.08</a>, and all kindergarten students.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.1158.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.5.6.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.19.3.0">2014 c 312 art 19 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.27.4.0">2016 c 189 art 27 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/18/#laws.0.2.0">2023 c 18 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.9.4.0">2023 c 55 art 9 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.26.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.42.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.116"><b>124D.116</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.7.3.0">1999 c 241 art 7 s 3</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.117">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.117 DISTRICTS TO OFFER SCHOOL BREAKFAST PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.117.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Breakfast required.</span></h2>
          <p>A district must offer a school breakfast program in every school building in which at least 33 percent of the school lunches served during the second preceding school year were served free or at a reduced price.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.117.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>Subdivision 1 does not apply to a school in which fewer than 25 pupils are expected to take part in the program. It also does not apply to a district that does not participate in the national school lunch program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.117.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.8.5.0">1989 c 329 art 8 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.8.27.0">1990 c 562 art 8 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.8.6.0">1991 c 265 art 8 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.8.8.0">1994 c 647 art 8 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.80.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 80</a>,164</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.118">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.118 SCHOOL MILK PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.118.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Legislative findings.</span></h2>
          <p>The legislature finds that for best health and well-being, school children in the state should receive at least one serving of milk each day. The school milk program established in this section is to provide districts in the state with added resources so that all kindergarten students in public and nonpublic schools may have access to wholesome milk on a daily basis.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.118.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment; school participation.</span></h2>
          <p>Each district in the state is encouraged to participate in the state-supported school milk program for kindergartners. Participating districts must provide one serving of milk on each school day to each kindergarten student attending a public or nonpublic school in the district. No student is required to accept the milk that is provided by the district. The program must be promoted and operated under the direction of the commissioner or the commissioner's designee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.118.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Program guidelines; duties of the commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) encourage all districts to participate in the school milk program for kindergartners;</p>
          <p>(2) prepare program guidelines, not subject to chapter 14 until July 1, 1998, which will effectively and efficiently distribute appropriated and donated money to participating districts; and</p>
          <p>(3) seek donations and matching funds from appropriate private and public sources.</p>
          <p>(b) Program guidelines may provide for disbursement to districts through a mechanism of prepayments or by reimbursement for approved program expenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.118.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">In accordance with program guidelines, the commissioner shall reimburse each participating public or nonpublic school 20 cents for each half-pint of milk that is served to kindergarten students and is not part of a school lunch or breakfast reimbursed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.111">124D.111</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.1158">124D.1158</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.118.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/688/#laws.16.1.0">1988 c 688 art 16 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/187/#laws.4.4.0">1997 c 187 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.81.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 81</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.6.27.0">1998 c 398 art 6 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.5.7.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.5.4.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 5 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/363/#laws.2.12.0">2008 c 363 art 2 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.119">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.119 SUMMER FOOD SERVICE PROGRAM AND CHILD AND ADULT CARE FOOD PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.119.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Summer Food Service Program replacement aid.</span></h2>
          <p>State funds are available to compensate department-approved Summer Food Service Program sponsors. Reimbursement shall be made on December 15 based on total meals served by each sponsor from the end of the school year to the beginning of the next school year on a pro rata basis.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.119.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Child and Adult Care Food Program and Summer Food Service Program sponsor organizations.</span></h2>
          <p>Legally distinct Child and Adult Care Food Program and Summer Food Service Program sites may transfer sponsoring organizations no more than once per year, except under extenuating circumstances including termination of the sponsoring organization's agreement or other circumstances approved by the Department of Education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.119.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Child and Adult Care Food Program training.</span></h2>
          <p>Prior to applying to sponsor a Child and Adult Care Food Program site, a nongovernmental organization applicant must provide documentation to the Department of Education verifying that staff members have completed program-specific training as designated by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.119.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Summer Food Service Program training.</span></h2>
          <p>Summer Food Service Program providers, including new applicants, must comply with all applicable federal training requirements for their staff.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.119.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Summer Food Service Program locations.</span></h2>
          <p>Consistent with Code of Federal Regulations, title 7, section 225.6(d)(1)(ii), the Department of Education must not approve a new Summer Food Service Program open site that is within a half-mile radius of an existing Summer Food Service Program open site. The department may approve a new Summer Food Service Program open site within a half-mile radius only if the new program will not be serving the same group of children for the same meal type or if there are safety issues that could present barriers to participation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.119.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.6.13.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 6 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.7.164.0">1998 c 397 art 7 s 164</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.6.26.0">1998 c 398 art 6 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.7.3.0">2013 c 116 art 7 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.9.5.0">2023 c 55 art 9 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.1191">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.1191 DONATIONS TO FOOD SHELF PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <p>Schools and community organizations participating in any federal child nutrition meal program may donate food to food shelf programs, provided that the food shelf:</p>
        <p>(1) is a nonprofit corporation or is affiliated with a nonprofit corporation, as defined in section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986;</p>
        <p>(2) distributes food without charge to needy individuals;</p>
        <p>(3) does not limit food distributions to individuals of a particular religious affiliation, race, or other criteria unrelated to need; and</p>
        <p>(4) has a stable address and directly serves individuals.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.1191.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.5.2.0">2014 c 272 art 5 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.1195">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.1195 COMMODITY DONATED FOOD REVOLVING FUND.</h1>
        <p>A revolving fund is established for the purpose of depositing cash received for commodity donated foods that have been lost, damaged, recalled, or diverted for processing. The state shall use the fund to issue payments for the value of the lost, damaged, recalled, or diverted commodity donated foods and related costs.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.1195.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.5.3.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 5 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">LEARNING YEAR PROGRAMS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.12">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.12 PURPOSE OF FLEXIBLE LEARNING YEAR PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.12">124D.12</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.127">124D.127</a> authorize districts to evaluate, plan and employ the use of flexible learning year programs. It is anticipated that the open selection of the type of flexible learning year operation from a variety of alternatives will allow each district seeking to utilize this concept to suitably fulfill the educational needs of its pupils. These alternatives must include, but not be limited to, various 45-15 plans, four-quarter plans, quinmester plans, extended learning year plans, and flexible all-year plans. The commissioner must establish clear criteria for evaluating a district's application to use a four-day school week plan, at least annually accept district applications to use a four-day school week plan, and determine whether each application meets the criteria. The commissioner must give a school district one school year's notice before revoking approval of its flexible learning year program. Approval of a four-day school week plan may not be revoked for six years from the date it is granted.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.12.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/326/#laws.0.2.0">1974 c 326 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.4.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.1.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 1</a>,124; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.1.2.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.1.5.0">2024 c 109 art 1 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.121">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.121 DEFINITION OF FLEXIBLE LEARNING YEAR PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>"Flexible learning year program" means any district plan approved by the commissioner that utilizes buildings and facilities during the entire year or that provides forms of optional scheduling of pupils and personnel during the learning year in elementary and secondary schools or residential facilities for children with a disability.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.121.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/326/#laws.0.3.0">1974 c 326 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.38.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 38</a>; art 9 s 5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.2.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 2</a>,124; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.122">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.122 ESTABLISHMENT OF FLEXIBLE LEARNING YEAR PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>The board of any district or a consortium of districts, with the approval of the commissioner, may establish and operate a flexible learning year program in one or more of the day or residential facilities for children with a disability within the district. Consortiums may use a single application and evaluation process, though results, public hearings, and board approvals must be obtained for each district as required under appropriate sections. The commissioner must approve or disapprove of a flexible learning year application within 45 business days of receiving the application. If the commissioner disapproves the application, the commissioner must give the district or consortium detailed reasons for the disapproval.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.122.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/326/#laws.0.4.0">1974 c 326 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.38.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 38</a>; art 9 s 6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.3.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 3</a>,124; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.3.23.0">2013 c 116 art 3 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.1.3.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 1 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.123">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.123 DIVISION OF CHILDREN INTO GROUPS.</h1>
        <p>The board of any district operating a flexible learning year program in one or more of the facilities within the district must divide the students of each selected facility into as many groups as necessary to accommodate this program. Students of the same family must be placed in the same group unless one or more of these students is enrolled in a special education class or unless the parent or guardian of these students requests that the students be placed in different groups. No board may discriminate on the basis of race, color, creed, religion, marital status, status with regard to public assistance, sex, or national origin when assigning pupils to attendance groups pursuant to this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.123.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/326/#laws.0.5.0">1974 c 326 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.7.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.4.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 4</a>,124</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.124">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.124 PUBLIC HEARING BEFORE IMPLEMENTATION.</h1>
        <p>Before implementing a flexible learning year program in any facility of the district, the board must negotiate with the teachers, principals, assistant principals, supervisory personnel and employees to the extent required by the Public Employment Labor Relations Act, and must consult with the parents of pupils who would be affected by the change, and with the community at large. These procedures must include at least three informational meetings for which the board has given published notice to the teachers and employees and to the parents of pupils affected.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.124.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/326/#laws.0.6.0">1974 c 326 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.8.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.5.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 5</a>,124</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.125">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.125 ASSIGNMENT OF TEACHERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.125.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Implementing program.</span></h2>
          <p>In districts where a flexible learning year program is implemented in fewer than all of the facilities maintained by the district, the board of the district must make every reasonable effort to assign qualified teachers who prefer a traditional schedule to facilities of the same level retaining a traditional schedule.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.125.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Teacher schedule.</span></h2>
          <p>A full-time teacher currently employed by a district that converts to a flexible learning year program may not, without the teacher's written consent, be required to teach under this program (1) more or less than the number of scheduled days or their equivalent the facilities of the district were maintained during the year preceding implementation of the flexible learning year program; (2) in a period of the calendar year substantially different from the period in which the teacher taught during the year preceding implementation of the flexible learning year program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.125.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contract rights; program adoption.</span></h2>
          <p>In no event may a teacher's continuing contract rights to a position held the year preceding implementation of a flexible learning year program or teaching experience earned during a probationary period the year preceding implementation be lost or impaired upon adoption of a flexible learning year program. If the year of teaching preceding implementation was the end of a probationary period, the continuing contract right to a full year's contract which normally would be acquired for the next succeeding learning year must be acquired in the year of adoption of the flexible program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.125.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Contract for learning year.</span></h2>
          <p>Any district operating a flexible learning year program must enter into one contract governing the entire learning year with each teacher employed in a flexible program. If individual teachers contract to teach less than a period of 175 days during a learning year, each 175 days of employment accrued during any five-year period after the adoption of a flexible learning year program must be deemed consecutive and constitutes a full year's employment for purposes of establishing and retaining continuing contract rights to a full learning year position pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a>, subdivisions 5 and 7, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41">122A.41</a>, subdivisions 2 and 4. A teacher who has not been discharged or advised of a refusal to renew the teacher's contract by the applicable date, as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41">122A.41</a>, in the year in which the teacher will complete the requisite number of days for securing a continuing contract must have a continuing full learning year contract with the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.125.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Contract rights; termination of program.</span></h2>
          <p>Continuing contract rights established pursuant to this section must not be impaired or lost by the termination of a flexible learning year program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.125.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/326/#laws.0.7.0">1974 c 326 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/764/#laws.0.6.0">1978 c 764 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.9.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.6.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 6</a>,124; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.126">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.126 POWERS AND DUTIES OF COMMISSIONER; FLEXIBLE LEARNING YEAR PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.126.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Powers and duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must:</p>
          <p>(1) promulgate rules necessary to the operation of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.12">124D.12</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.127">124D.127</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) cooperate with and provide supervision of flexible learning year programs to determine compliance with the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.12">124D.12</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.127">124D.127</a>, the commissioner's standards and qualifications, and the proposed program as submitted and approved;</p>
          <p>(3) provide any necessary adjustments of (a) attendance and membership computations and (b) the dates and percentages of apportionment of state aids; and</p>
          <p>(4) consistent with the definition of "average daily membership" in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.8">126C.05, subdivision 8</a>, furnish the board of a district implementing a flexible learning year program with a formula for computing average daily membership. This formula must be computed so that tax levies to be made by the district, state aids to be received by the district, and any and all other formulas based upon average daily membership are not affected solely as a result of adopting this plan of instruction.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.126.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.12">124D.12</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.127">124D.127</a> may not be construed to authorize the commissioner to require the establishment of a flexible learning year program in any district in which the board has not voted to establish, maintain, and operate such a program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.126.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/326/#laws.0.9.0">1974 c 326 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/706/#laws.0.3.0">1978 c 706 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.11.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.7.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 7</a>,124; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.127">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.127 TERMINATION OF FLEXIBLE LEARNING YEAR PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>The board of any district, with the approval of the commissioner of education, may terminate a flexible learning year program in one or more of the day or residential facilities for children with a disability within the district. This section shall not be construed to permit an exception to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22">120A.22</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.41#stat.127A.41.7">127A.41, subdivision 7</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.43">127A.43</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.127.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/326/#laws.0.10.0">1974 c 326 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/209/#laws.2.1.0">1989 c 209 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.38.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 38</a>; art 9 s 12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.124.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 124</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.128">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.128 LEARNING YEAR PROGRAM TO PROVIDE INSTRUCTION THROUGHOUT YEAR.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.128.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program established.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A learning year program provides instruction throughout the year on an extended year calendar, extended school day calendar, or both. A learning year program may begin after the close of the regular school year in June. The program may be for students in one or more grade levels from kindergarten through grade 12.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.128.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner designation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A state-approved alternative program designated by the state must be a site. A state-approved alternative program must provide services to students who meet the criteria in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.68">124D.68</a> and who are enrolled in:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a district that is served by the state-approved alternative program; or</p>
          <p class="in">(2) a charter school located within the geographic boundaries of a district that is served by the state-approved alternative program.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) To be designated, a state-approved alternative program must demonstrate to the commissioner that it will:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) provide a program of instruction that permits pupils to receive instruction throughout the entire year; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) develop and maintain a separate record system that, for purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05">126C.05</a>, permits identification of membership attributable to pupils participating in the program. The record system and identification must ensure that the program will not have the effect of increasing the total average daily membership attributable to an individual pupil as a result of a learning year program. The record system must include the date the pupil originally enrolled in a learning year program, the pupil's grade level, the date of each grade promotion, the average daily membership generated in each grade level, the number of credits or standards earned, and the number needed to graduate.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) A student who has not completed a school district's graduation requirements may continue to enroll in courses the student must complete in order to graduate until the student satisfies the district's graduation requirements or the student is 21 years old, whichever comes first. A student with a disability as set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.02">125A.02</a> may continue to enroll in courses until the student graduates with a regular high school diploma or the student is 22 years old, whichever comes first.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.128.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Student planning.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">A district, charter school, or state-approved alternative program must inform all pupils and their parents about the learning year program and that participation in the program is optional. A continual learning plan must be developed at least annually for each pupil with the participation of the pupil, parent or guardian, teachers, and other staff; each participant must sign and date the plan. The plan must specify the learning experiences that must occur during the entire fiscal year and are necessary for grade progression or, for secondary students, graduation. The plan must include:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) the pupil's learning objectives and experiences, including courses or credits the pupil plans to complete each year and, for a secondary pupil, the graduation requirements the student must complete;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the assessment measurements used to evaluate a pupil's objectives;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) requirements for grade level or other appropriate progression; and</p>
          <p class="in">(4) for pupils generating more than one average daily membership in a given grade, an indication of which objectives were unmet.</p>
          <p class="bl">The plan may be modified to conform to district schedule changes. The district may not modify the plan if the modification would result in delaying the student's time of graduation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.128.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.51.0">2000 c 254 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.128.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>A district may contract with a licensed employee to provide services in a learning year program that are in addition to the services provided according to the master contract of employment for teachers or an equivalent contract for licensed employees who are not teachers. These additional services and compensation, if any, for the services must not become a part of the employee's continuing contract rights under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41">122A.41</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.128.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Revenue computation and reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>Aid and levy revenue computations must be based on the total number of hours of education programs for pupils in average daily membership for each fiscal year. Average daily membership shall be computed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.15">126C.05, subdivision 15</a>. Hours of participation that occur after the close of the regular instructional year and before July 1 must be attributed to the following fiscal year. For revenue computation purposes, the learning year program shall generate revenue based on the formulas for the fiscal year in which the services are provided. The dates a participating pupil is promoted must be reported in a timely manner to the department.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.128.6a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6a.<span class="headnote">Process to address audit findings.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If, during an audit of a district's learning year program, the commissioner finds that the district is not meeting program requirements, the commissioner must notify the board of that district in writing. The notice must specify the findings in detail, describe the correction required, set a reasonable time during which the findings should be corrected, and advise that general education revenue to the district may be reduced. The commissioner may extend the time allowed for the correction.</p>
          <p>(b) A board that receives a notice under paragraph (a) may decide by majority vote of the entire board to dispute that:</p>
          <p>(1) the specified finding exists;</p>
          <p>(2) the time allowed is reasonable; or</p>
          <p>(3) the commissioner should reduce district general education revenue.</p>
          <p>The board must give the commissioner written notice of the board's decision within 30 days of receipt of the audit report. After making any further investigations the commissioner deems necessary, the commissioner must decide whether or not to adhere to the commissioner's original notice and must notify the board of the commissioner's decision.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner may reduce or withhold state general education revenues as the result of an audit. The commissioner may decide not to reduce or withhold state general education revenues if the district corrects the specified finding, or after receiving the district's notice disputing the finding, the commissioner decides the finding does not exist.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.128.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.78.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 78</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.128.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.9.5.0">1989 c 329 art 9 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.37.0">1991 c 130 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.7.3.0">1991 c 265 art 7 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.8.2.0">1992 c 499 art 8 s 2</a>; art 12 s 29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.12.8.0">1993 c 224 art 12 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.8.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 8</a>-10,124; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.10.4.0">2000 c 489 art 10 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.29.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 29</a>-33; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.1.2.0">2007 c 146 art 1 s 2</a>-4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.2.43.0">2009 c 96 art 2 s 43</a>,44; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.13.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.2.50.0">2023 c 55 art 2 s 50</a>; art 7 s 5</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.129">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.129 EDUCATE PARENTS PARTNERSHIP.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may work in partnership with health care providers and community organizations to provide parent information to parents of newborns at the time of birth. The commissioner may coordinate the partnership and the distribution of informational material to the parents of newborns before they leave the hospital with early childhood organizations, including, but not limited to, early childhood family education, child care resource and referral, and interagency early intervention committees. The commissioner may develop a resource website that promotes, at a minimum, the department website for information and links to resources on child development, parent education, child care, and consumer safety information.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.129.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.2.6.0">2006 c 282 art 2 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>

        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.15.1">

          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.05#stat.142D.05.1">142D.05, subdivision 1</a>]</p>

          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.7.21.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 7 s 21</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.15.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
