      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.162">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.162 KINDERGARTEN ENTRY ASSESSMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.162.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Assessment required.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of education must implement a kindergarten entry assessment of incoming kindergartners to identify the percent of kindergartners who meet or exceed end-of-year prekindergarten early learning standards.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.162.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Process.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) School districts and charter schools must choose a kindergarten entry assessment tool from a menu of valid and reliable measurement instruments approved by the department that:</p>
          <p>(1) are aligned to the state early childhood indicators of progress and kindergarten standards and are based on the criteria to be an early learning assessment approved by the department;</p>
          <p>(2) support the comprehensive achievement and civic readiness goals in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11#stat.120B.11.1">120B.11, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c); and</p>
          <p>(3) are based, in part, on information collected from teachers, early learning professionals, families, and other partners.</p>
          <p>(b) The department must provide technical assistance and professional development related to the assessment required under this section to educators, school districts, and charter schools.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.162.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reporting.</span></h2>
          <p>School districts and charter schools must annually report the results of kindergarten entry assessments to the department in a form and manner determined by the commissioner that is concurrent with a district's and charter school's comprehensive achievement and civic readiness report under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11#stat.120B.11.5">120B.11, subdivision 5</a>. The commissioner must publicly report kindergarten readiness results as part of the performance reports required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.36">120B.36</a> and in a manner consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.35#stat.120B.35.3">120B.35, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.162.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Implementation.</span></h2>
          <p>The requirements under this section must be phased in over three school years with all school districts and charter schools complying beginning with the 2025-2026 school year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.162.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/282/#laws.2.8.0">2006 c 282 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/54/#laws.0.10.0">2023 c 54 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.2.31.0">2024 c 109 art 2 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.165"><b>124D.165</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.25">142D.25</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.166">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.166 LIMIT ON SCREEN TIME FOR CHILDREN IN PRESCHOOL AND KINDERGARTEN.</h1>
        <p>A child in a publicly funded preschool or kindergarten program may not use an individual-use screen, such as a tablet, smartphone, or other digital media, without engagement from a teacher or other students. This section does not apply to a child for whom the school has an individualized family service plan, an individualized education program, or a 504 plan in effect.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.166.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/13/#laws.9.2.0">1Sp2021 c 13 art 9 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.17"><b>124D.17</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.7.12.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 7 s 12</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.175"><b>124D.175</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.9.19.0">2007 c 146 art 9 s 19</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.18">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.18 PURPOSE OF COMMUNITY EDUCATION PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <p>The purpose of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.18">124D.18</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.19">124D.19</a> is to make maximum use of the public schools of Minnesota by the community and to expand utilization by the school of the human resources of the community, by establishing a community education program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.18.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/900/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 900 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/239/#laws.0.26.0">1976 c 239 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.4.22.0">1980 c 609 art 4 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.103.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 103</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.19">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.19 COMMUNITY EDUCATION PROGRAMS; ADVISORY COUNCIL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>Each school board may initiate a community education program in its district and provide for the general supervision of the program. Each board may, as it considers appropriate, employ community education staff to further the purposes of the community education program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Advisory council.</span></h2>
          <p>Each board must provide for an advisory council to consist of members who represent: various service organizations; churches; public and nonpublic schools; local government including elected officials; public and private nonprofit agencies serving youth and families; parents; youth; park, recreation or forestry services of municipal or local government units located in whole or in part within the boundaries of the school district; and any other groups participating in the community education program in the school district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Community education director.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided under paragraphs (b) and (c), each board shall employ a licensed community education director. The board shall submit the name of the person who is serving as director of community education under this section on the district's annual community education report to the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) A board may apply to the Minnesota Board of School Administrators under Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/3512.5300">3512.5300</a>, subpart 11, for authority to use an individual who is not licensed as a community education director.</p>
          <p>(c) A board of a district with a total population of 6,000 or less may identify an employee who holds a valid superintendent license under Minnesota Rules, chapter 3512, to serve as director of community education. To be eligible for an exception under this paragraph, the board shall certify in writing to the commissioner that the district has not placed a licensed director of community education on unrequested leave. A principal serving as a community education director under this paragraph on June 1, 2011, may continue to serve in that capacity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Cooperation.</span></h2>
          <p>The council must function in cooperation with the community education director in an advisory capacity in the interest of promoting the goals and objectives of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.18">124D.18</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.19">124D.19</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Policy to avoid program duplication.</span></h2>
          <p>Each council must adopt a policy to reduce and eliminate program duplication within the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Summer programs.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, during the summer a school district may offer community education programs to elementary and secondary pupils. The district may use community education revenue received pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.20">124D.20</a> and charge fees for the cost of the programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Programs for adults with disabilities.</span></h2>
          <p>A board may offer, as part of a community education program, a program for adults with disabilities. Boards are encouraged to offer programs cooperatively with other districts and organizations. Programs may not be limited to district residents. Programs may include:</p>
          <p>(1) services enabling the adults to participate in community activities or community education classes;</p>
          <p>(2) classes specifically for adults with disabilities;</p>
          <p>(3) outreach activities to identify adults needing service;</p>
          <p>(4) activities to increase public awareness of the roles of people with disabilities;</p>
          <p>(5) activities to enhance the role of people with disabilities in the community; and</p>
          <p>(6) other direct and indirect services and activities benefiting adults with disabilities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Program approval.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Beginning July 1, 2024, and at least once every five years thereafter, a district's community education advisory council must review and approve the district's adults with disabilities program and submit a statement of assurances to the commissioner in the form and manner determined by the commissioner. The program must seek feedback from adults with disabilities and other community organizations providing services to adults with disabilities.</p>
          <p>(b) Each school district with an adults with disabilities program must include at least the following information about its adults with disabilities program in its annual community education report under subdivision 14:</p>
          <p>(1) a summary of the characteristics of the people served by the program;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of the program services and activities;</p>
          <p>(3) the most recent program budget;</p>
          <p>(4) a summary of the participation by adults with disabilities in developing the program;</p>
          <p>(5) an assessment of the needs of adults with disabilities; and</p>
          <p>(6) a description of cooperative efforts with community organizations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Youth development plans.</span></h2>
          <p>A district advisory council may prepare a youth development plan. The council is encouraged to use the state guidelines when developing the local plan. The school board may approve the youth development plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Youth service programs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A school board may offer, as part of a community education program with a youth development program, a youth service program that provides young people with meaningful opportunities to become involved in their community, develop individual capabilities, make career connections, seek support networks and services, become active citizens, and address community needs through youth service. The board may award up to one credit, or the equivalent, toward graduation for a pupil who completes the youth service requirements of the district. The community education advisory council, after considering the results of the commissioner's study under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.50#stat.124D.50.1">124D.50, subdivision 1</a>, must design the program in cooperation with the district planning, evaluating and reporting committee and local organizations that train volunteers or need volunteers' services.</p>
          <p>(b) Programs must include:</p>
          <p>(1) preliminary training for pupil volunteers conducted, when possible, by organizations experienced in such training;</p>
          <p>(2) supervision of the pupil volunteers to ensure appropriate placement and adequate learning opportunity;</p>
          <p>(3) sufficient opportunity, in a positive setting for human development, for pupil volunteers to develop general skills in preparation for employment, to enhance self-esteem and self-worth, and to give genuine service to their community;</p>
          <p>(4) integration of academic learning with the service experience; and</p>
          <p>(5) integration of youth community service with elementary and secondary curriculum.</p>
          <p>(c) Youth service projects include, but are not limited to, the following:</p>
          <p>(1) human services for the elderly, including home care and related services;</p>
          <p>(2) tutoring and mentoring;</p>
          <p>(3) training for and providing emergency services;</p>
          <p>(4) services at extended day programs;</p>
          <p>(5) environmental services; and</p>
          <p>(6) service-learning programs in which schools, including postsecondary schools, and employers work together with young people to provide them with meaningful opportunities for community service and with the academic and technical skills that employers require.</p>
          <p>(d) A youth service project must have a community sponsor that may be a governmental unit or nonprofit organization. To assure that pupils provide additional services, each sponsor must assure that pupil services do not displace employees or reduce the workload of any employee.</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner shall assist districts in planning youth service programs, implementing programs, and developing recommendations for obtaining community sponsors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">School-age care programs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A school board may offer, as part of a community education program, a school-age care program for children from kindergarten through grade 6 for the purpose of expanding students' learning opportunities. If the school board chooses not to offer a school-age care program, it may allow an appropriate insured community group, for profit entity or nonprofit organization to use available school facilities for the purpose of offering a school-age care program.</p>
          <p>(b) A school-age care program must include the following:</p>
          <p>(1) adult supervised programs while school is not in session;</p>
          <p>(2) parental involvement in program design and direction;</p>
          <p>(3) partnerships with the kindergarten through grade 12 system, and other public, private, or nonprofit entities;</p>
          <p>(4) opportunities for trained secondary school pupils to work with younger children in a supervised setting as part of a community service program; and</p>
          <p>(5) access to available school facilities, including the gymnasium, sports equipment, computer labs, and media centers, when not otherwise in use as part of the operation of the school. The school district may establish reasonable rules relating to access to these facilities and may require that:</p>
          <p>(i) the organization request access to the facilities and prepare and maintain a schedule of proposed use;</p>
          <p>(ii) the organization provide evidence of adequate insurance to cover the activities to be conducted in the facilities; and</p>
          <p>(iii) the organization prepare and maintain a plan demonstrating the adequacy and training of staff to supervise the use of the facilities.</p>
          <p>(c) The district may charge a sliding fee based upon family income for school-age care programs. The district may receive money from other public or private sources for the school-age care program. The board of the district must develop standards for school-age child care programs. The commissioner of education may not adopt rules for school-age care programs.</p>
          <p>(d) The district shall maintain a separate account within the community services fund for all funds related to the school-age care program.</p>
          <p>(e) A district is encouraged to coordinate the school-age care program with its special education, vocational education, adult basic education, early childhood family education programs, kindergarten through grade 12 instruction and curriculum services, youth development and youth service agencies, and with related services provided by other governmental agencies and nonprofit agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Youth after-school enrichment programs.</span></h2>
          <p>Each district operating a community education program under this section may establish a youth after-school enrichment program to maintain and expand participation by school-age youth in supervised activities during nonschool hours. The youth after-school enrichment programs must include activities that support development of social, mental, physical, and creative abilities of school-age youth; provide structured youth programs during high-risk times; and design programming to promote youth leadership development and improved academic performance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Youth after-school enrichment program goals.</span></h2>
          <p>The goals of youth after-school enrichment programs are to:</p>
          <p>(1) collaborate with and leverage existing community resources that have demonstrated effectiveness;</p>
          <p>(2) reach out to children and youth, including at-risk youth, in the community;</p>
          <p>(3) increase the number of children participating in adult-supervised programs during nonschool hours;</p>
          <p>(4) support academic achievement; and</p>
          <p>(5) increase skills in technology, the arts, sports, and other activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.19.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Community education; annual report.</span></h2>
          <p>Each district offering a community education program under this section must annually report to the department information regarding each community education program, including youth after-school enrichment programs, that receives aid or levy.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.19.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/900/#laws.0.4.0">1971 c 900 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/239/#laws.0.29.0">1976 c 239 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/447/#laws.4.1.0">1977 c 447 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.4.22.0">1980 c 609 art 4 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/548/#laws.6.1.0">1982 c 548 art 6 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/12/#laws.4.1.0">1Sp1985 c 12 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.4.3.0">1987 c 398 art 4 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.4.1.0">1988 c 718 art 4 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.4.1.0">1989 c 329 art 4 s 1</a>,2; art 9 s 6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.3.1.0">1990 c 562 art 3 s 1</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.2.8.0">1991 c 199 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.4.1.0">1991 c 265 art 4 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.13.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.12.9.0">1993 c 224 art 12 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.2.11.0">1994 c 465 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.30.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 30</a>; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/162/#laws.2.12.0">1997 c 162 art 2 s 12</a>-14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.36.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 36</a>-42,103; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/205/#laws.1.44.0">1999 c 205 art 1 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/3/#laws.2.8.0">1Sp2001 c 3 art 2 s 8</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.8.1.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/294/#laws.5.7.0">2004 c 294 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.6.6.0">2009 c 96 art 6 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.8.1.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/40/#laws.1.14.0">2017 c 40 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/25/#laws.0.43.0">2023 c 25 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.7.1.0">2024 c 115 art 7 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.20">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.20 COMMUNITY EDUCATION REVENUE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Total community education revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Total community education revenue equals the sum of a district's general community education revenue, youth service program revenue, and youth after-school enrichment revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>To be eligible for community education revenue, a district must operate a community education program that complies with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.19">124D.19</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">General community education revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>The general community education revenue for a district equals $5.42 for fiscal year 2007 through fiscal year 2024 and $6.35 for fiscal year 2025 and later, times the greater of 1,335 or the population of the district. The population of the district is determined according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/275.14">275.14</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Youth service revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Youth service program revenue is available to a district that has implemented a youth development plan and a youth service program. Youth service revenue equals $1 times the greater of 1,335 or the population of the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.4a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4a.<span class="headnote">Youth after-school enrichment revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>In fiscal year 2003 and thereafter, youth after-school enrichment revenue for a district operating a youth after-school enrichment program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.19#stat.124D.19.12">124D.19, subdivision 12</a>, equals:</p>
          <p>(1) $1.85 times the greater of 1,335 or the population of the district, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/275.14">275.14</a>, not to exceed 10,000; and</p>
          <p>(2) $0.43 times the population of the district, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/275.14">275.14</a>, in excess of 10,000. Youth after-school enrichment revenue must be reserved for youth after-school enrichment programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Total community education levy.</span></h2>
          <p>To obtain total community education revenue, a district may levy the amount raised by a maximum tax rate of 0.94 percent through fiscal year 2024, 0.375 percent in fiscal year 2025, 0.3298 percent in fiscal year 2026, and 0.3128 percent in fiscal year 2027 and later, times the adjusted net tax capacity of the district. If the amount of the total community education levy would exceed the total community education revenue, the total community education levy shall be determined according to subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Community education levy; districts off the formula.</span></h2>
          <p>If the amount of the community education levy for a district exceeds the district's community education revenue, the amount of the community education levy is limited to the district's community education revenue according to subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Community education aid.</span></h2>
          <p>A district's community education aid is the difference between its community education revenue and the community education levy. If the district does not levy the entire amount permitted, the community education aid must be reduced in proportion to the actual amount levied.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Uses of general revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) General community education revenue may be used for:</p>
          <p>(1) nonvocational, recreational, and leisure time activities and programs;</p>
          <p>(2) programs for adults with disabilities, if the programs and budgets are approved by the department;</p>
          <p>(3) adult basic education programs, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.52">124D.52</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) summer programs for elementary and secondary pupils;</p>
          <p>(5) implementation of a youth development plan;</p>
          <p>(6) implementation of a youth service program;</p>
          <p>(7) early childhood family education programs, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.10">142D.10</a>;</p>
          <p>(8) school readiness programs, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.05">142D.05</a>; and</p>
          <p>(9) school-age care programs, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.19#stat.124D.19.11">124D.19, subdivision 11</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to money from other sources, a district may use up to ten percent of its community education revenue for equipment that is used exclusively in community education programs. This revenue may be used only for the following purposes:</p>
          <p>(1) to purchase or lease computers and related materials;</p>
          <p>(2) to purchase or lease equipment for instructional programs; and</p>
          <p>(3) to purchase textbooks and library books.</p>
          <p>(c) General community education revenue must not be used to subsidize the direct activity costs for adult enrichment programs. Direct activity costs include, but are not limited to, the cost of the activity leader or instructor, cost of materials, or transportation costs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Use of youth service revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Youth service revenue may be used to implement a youth development plan approved by the school board and to provide a youth service program according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.19#stat.124D.19.10">124D.19, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Reserve account.</span></h2>
          <p>Community education revenue, which includes aids, levies, fees, grants, and all other revenues received by the school district for community education programs, must be maintained in a reserve account within the community service fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/239/#laws.3.6.0">2012 c 239 art 3 s 6</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.20.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.</h2>
          <p>MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/239/#laws.3.6.0">2012 c 239 art 3 s 6</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.20.history
			">

<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.11.1.0">2023 c 55 art 11 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.26.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.42.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 42</a></p>

      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.21"><b>124D.21</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.8.8.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 8 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.22">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.22 SCHOOL-AGE CARE REVENUE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.22.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>A district that offers a school-age care program according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.19#stat.124D.19.11">124D.19, subdivision 11</a>, is eligible for school-age care revenue for the additional costs of providing services to children with disabilities or to children experiencing family or related problems of a temporary nature who participate in the school-age care program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.22.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">School-age care revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>The school-age care revenue for an eligible district equals the approved additional cost of providing services to children with disabilities or children experiencing family or related problems of a temporary nature who participate in the school-age care program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.22.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">School-age care levy.</span></h2>
          <p>To obtain school-age care revenue, a school district may levy an amount equal to the district's school-age care revenue as defined in subdivision 2 multiplied by the lesser of one, or the ratio of the quotient derived by dividing the adjusted net tax capacity of the district for the year before the year the levy is certified by the resident pupil units in the district for the school year to which the levy is attributable, to $2,318.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.22.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">School-age care aid.</span></h2>
          <p>A district's school-age care aid is the difference between its school-age care revenue and its school-age care levy. If a district does not levy the entire amount permitted, school-age care aid must be reduced in proportion to the actual amount levied.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.22.history
			">

        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.4.8.0">1992 c 499 art 4 s 8</a>; art 12 s 29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.4.32.0">1993 c 224 art 4 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/162/#laws.2.24.0">1997 c 162 art 2 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.80.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 80</a>,81,103; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/205/#laws.1.45.0">1999 c 205 art 1 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.8.6.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 8 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/292/#laws.1.7.0">2012 c 292 art 1 s 7</a></p>

      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.231">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.231 FULL-SERVICE COMMUNITY SCHOOLS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.231.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(a) "Community organization" means a nonprofit organization that has been in existence for three years or more and serves persons within the community surrounding the covered school site on education and other issues.</p>
          <p>(b) "Community school consortium" means a group of schools and community organizations that propose to work together to plan and implement community school programming.</p>
          <p>(c) "Community school programming" means services, activities, and opportunities described under subdivision 2, paragraph (f).</p>
          <p>(d) "Community-wide full-service community school leadership team" means a district-level team that is responsible for guiding the vision, policy, resource alignment, implementation, oversight, and goal setting for community school programs within the district. This team shall include representatives from the district, including teachers, school leaders, students, and family members from the eligible schools; community members; system-level partners that include representatives from government agencies, relevant unions, and nonprofit and other community-based partners; and, if applicable, the full-service community school initiative director.</p>
          <p>(e) "Full-service community school initiative director" means a director responsible for coordinating districtwide administrative and leadership assistance to community school sites and site coordinators, including serving as chairperson for the district's community-wide full-service community school leadership team; site coordinator support; data gathering and evaluation; administration of partnership and data agreements, contracts, and procurement; and grant administration.</p>
          <p>(f) "High-quality child care or early childhood education programming" means educational programming for preschool-aged children that is grounded in research, consistent with best practices in the field, and provided by licensed teachers.</p>
          <p>(g) "School site" means a school site at which an applicant has proposed or has been funded to provide community school programming.</p>
          <p>(h) "Site coordinator" means a full-time staff member serving one eligible school who is responsible for the identification, implementation, and coordination of programming to address the needs of the school community identified in the baseline analysis.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.231.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Full-service community school program.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall provide funding to districts and charter schools with eligible school sites to plan, implement, and improve full-service community schools. Eligible school sites must meet one of the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) the school is on a development plan for continuous improvement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.35#stat.120B.35.2">120B.35, subdivision 2</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) the school is in a district that has an achievement and integration plan approved by the commissioner of education under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.861">124D.861</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.862">124D.862</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Districts and charter schools may receive up to:</p>
          <p>(1) $100,000 for each eligible school available for up to one year to fund planning activities, including convening a full-service community school leadership team, facilitating family and community stakeholder engagement, conducting a baseline analysis, and creating a full-service community school plan. At the end of this period, the school must submit a full-service community school plan pursuant to paragraphs (d) and (e); and</p>
          <p>(2) $200,000 annually for each eligible school for up to three years of implementation of a full-service community school plan, pursuant to paragraphs (f) and (g). School sites receiving funding under this section shall hire or contract with a partner agency to hire a site coordinator to coordinate services at each covered school site. Districts or charter schools receiving funding under this section for three or more schools shall provide or contract with a partner agency to provide a full-service community school initiative director.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall consider additional school factors when dispensing funds including: schools with significant populations of students receiving free or reduced-price meals; significant homeless and highly mobile rates; equity among urban, suburban, and greater Minnesota schools; and demonstrated success implementing full-service community school programming.</p>
          <p>(d) A school site must establish a full-service community school leadership team responsible for developing school-specific programming goals, assessing program needs, and overseeing the process of implementing expanded programming. The school leadership team shall have at least 12 members and shall meet the following requirements:</p>
          <p>(1) at least 30 percent of the members are parents, guardians, or students and 30 percent of the members are teachers at the school site and must include the school principal and representatives from partner agencies; and</p>
          <p>(2) the full-service community school leadership team must be responsible for overseeing the baseline analyses under paragraph (e) and the creation of a full-service community school plan under paragraphs (f) and (g). A full-service community school leadership team must meet at least quarterly and have ongoing responsibility for monitoring the development and implementation of full-service community school operations and programming at the school site and shall issue recommendations to schools on a regular basis and summarized in an annual report. These reports shall also be made available to the public at the school site and on school and district websites.</p>
          <p>(e) School sites must complete a baseline analysis prior to the creation of a full-service community school plan. The analysis shall include:</p>
          <p>(1) a baseline analysis of needs at the school site, led by the school leadership team, including the following elements:</p>
          <p>(i) identification of challenges facing the school;</p>
          <p>(ii) analysis of the student body, including:</p>
          <p>(A) number and percentage of students with disabilities and needs of these students;</p>
          <p>(B) number and percentage of students who are English learners and the needs of these students;</p>
          <p>(C) number of students who are homeless or highly mobile;</p>
          <p>(D) number and percentage of students receiving free or reduced-price meals and the needs of these students; and</p>
          <p>(E) number and percentage of students by race and ethnicity;</p>
          <p>(iii) analysis of enrollment and retention rates for students with disabilities, English learners, homeless and highly mobile students, and students receiving free or reduced-price meals;</p>
          <p>(iv) analysis of suspension and expulsion data, including the justification for such disciplinary actions and the degree to which particular populations, including but not limited to American Indian students and students of color, students with disabilities, students who are English learners, and students receiving free or reduced-price meals are represented among students subject to such actions;</p>
          <p>(v) analysis of school achievement data disaggregated by major demographic categories, including but not limited to race, ethnicity, English learner status, disability status, and free or reduced-price meals status;</p>
          <p>(vi) analysis of current parent engagement strategies and their success; and</p>
          <p>(vii) evaluation of the need for and availability of full-service community school activities, including, but not limited to:</p>
          <p>(A) integrated student supports that address out-of-school barriers to learning through partnerships with social and health service agencies and providers, and may include medical, dental, vision care, and mental health services or counselors to assist with housing, transportation, nutrition, immigration, or criminal justice issues;</p>
          <p>(B) expanded and enriched learning time and opportunities, including before-school, after-school, weekend, and summer programs that provide additional academic instruction, individualized academic support, enrichment activities, and learning opportunities that emphasize real-world learning and community problem solving and may include art, music, drama, creative writing, hands-on experience with engineering or science, tutoring and homework help, or recreational programs that enhance and are consistent with the school's curriculum;</p>
          <p>(C) active family and community engagement that brings students' families and the community into the school as partners in education and makes the school a neighborhood hub, providing adults with educational opportunities that may include adult English as a second language classes, computer skills, art, or other programs that bring community members into the school for meetings or events; and</p>
          <p>(D) collaborative leadership and practices that build a culture of professional learning, collective trust, and shared responsibility and include a school-based full-service community school leadership team, a full-service community school site coordinator, a full-service community school initiative director, a community-wide leadership team, other leadership or governance teams, teacher learning communities, or other staff to manage the joint work of school and community organizations;</p>
          <p>(2) a baseline analysis of community assets, including documentation of individuals in the community, faith-based organizations, community and neighborhood associations, colleges, hospitals, libraries, businesses, and social service agencies that may be able to provide support and resources; and</p>
          <p>(3) a baseline analysis of needs in the community surrounding the school, led by the school leadership team, including:</p>
          <p>(i) the need for high-quality, full-day child care and early childhood education programs;</p>
          <p>(ii) the need for physical and mental health care services for children and adults; and</p>
          <p>(iii) the need for job training and other adult education programming.</p>
          <p>(f) Each school site receiving funding under this section must develop a full-service community school plan that utilizes and aligns district and community assets and establishes services in at least two of the following types of programming:</p>
          <p>(1) early childhood:</p>
          <p>(i) early childhood education; and</p>
          <p>(ii) child care services;</p>
          <p>(2) academic:</p>
          <p>(i) academic support and enrichment activities, including expanded learning time;</p>
          <p>(ii) summer or after-school enrichment and learning experiences;</p>
          <p>(iii) job training, internship opportunities, and career counseling services;</p>
          <p>(iv) programs that provide assistance to students who have been chronically absent, truant, suspended, or expelled; and</p>
          <p>(v) specialized instructional support services;</p>
          <p>(3) parental involvement:</p>
          <p>(i) programs that promote parental involvement and family literacy;</p>
          <p>(ii) parent leadership development activities that empower and strengthen families and communities, provide volunteer opportunities, or promote inclusion in school-based leadership teams; and</p>
          <p>(iii) parenting education activities;</p>
          <p>(4) mental and physical health:</p>
          <p>(i) mentoring and other youth development programs, including peer mentoring and conflict mediation;</p>
          <p>(ii) juvenile crime prevention and rehabilitation programs;</p>
          <p>(iii) home visitation services by teachers and other professionals;</p>
          <p>(iv) developmentally appropriate physical education;</p>
          <p>(v) nutrition services;</p>
          <p>(vi) primary health and dental care; and</p>
          <p>(vii) mental health counseling services;</p>
          <p>(5) community involvement:</p>
          <p>(i) service and service-learning opportunities;</p>
          <p>(ii) adult education, including instruction in English as a second language; and</p>
          <p>(iii) homeless prevention services;</p>
          <p>(6) positive discipline practices; and</p>
          <p>(7) other programming designed to meet school and community needs identified in the baseline analysis and reflected in the full-service community school plan.</p>
          <p>(g) The full-service community school leadership team at each school site must develop a full-service community school plan detailing the steps the school leadership team will take, including:</p>
          <p>(1) timely establishment and consistent operation of the school leadership team;</p>
          <p>(2) maintenance of attendance records in all programming components;</p>
          <p>(3) maintenance of measurable data showing annual participation and the impact of programming on the participating children and adults;</p>
          <p>(4) documentation of meaningful and sustained collaboration between the school and community stakeholders, including local governmental units, civic engagement organizations, businesses, and social service providers;</p>
          <p>(5) establishment and maintenance of partnerships with institutions, such as universities, hospitals, museums, or not-for-profit community organizations to further the development and implementation of community school programming;</p>
          <p>(6) ensuring compliance with the district nondiscrimination policy; and</p>
          <p>(7) plan for school leadership team development.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.231.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Full-service community school review.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A full-service community school site must submit to the commissioner, and make available at the school site and online, a report describing efforts to integrate community school programming at each covered school site and the effect of the transition to a full-service community school on participating children and adults. This report shall include, but is not limited to, the following:</p>
          <p>(1) an assessment of the effectiveness of the school site in development or implementing the community school plan;</p>
          <p>(2) problems encountered in the design and execution of the community school plan, including identification of any federal, state, or local statute or regulation impeding program implementation;</p>
          <p>(3) the operation of the school leadership team and its contribution to successful execution of the community school plan;</p>
          <p>(4) recommendations for improving delivery of community school programming to students and families;</p>
          <p>(5) the number and percentage of students receiving community school programming who had not previously been served;</p>
          <p>(6) the number and percentage of nonstudent community members receiving community school programming who had not previously been served;</p>
          <p>(7) improvement in retention among students who receive community school programming;</p>
          <p>(8) improvement in academic achievement among students who receive community school programming;</p>
          <p>(9) changes in student's readiness to enter school, active involvement in learning and in their community, physical, social and emotional health, and student's relationship with the school and community environment;</p>
          <p>(10) an accounting of anticipated local budget savings, if any, resulting from the implementation of the program;</p>
          <p>(11) improvements to the frequency or depth of families' involvement with their children's education;</p>
          <p>(12) assessment of community stakeholder satisfaction;</p>
          <p>(13) assessment of institutional partner satisfaction;</p>
          <p>(14) the ability, or anticipated ability, of the school site and partners to continue to provide services in the absence of future funding under this section;</p>
          <p>(15) increases in access to services for students and their families; and</p>
          <p>(16) the degree of increased collaboration among participating agencies and private partners.</p>
          <p>(b) Reports submitted under this section shall be evaluated by the commissioner with respect to the following criteria:</p>
          <p>(1) the effectiveness of the school or the community school consortium in implementing the full-service community school plan, including the degree to which the school site navigated difficulties encountered in the design and operation of the full-service community school plan, including identification of any federal, state, or local statute or regulation impeding program implementation;</p>
          <p>(2) the extent to which the project has produced lessons about ways to improve delivery of community school programming to students;</p>
          <p>(3) the degree to which there has been an increase in the number or percentage of students and nonstudents receiving community school programming;</p>
          <p>(4) the degree to which there has been an improvement in retention of students and improvement in academic achievement among students receiving community school programming;</p>
          <p>(5) local budget savings, if any, resulting from the implementation of the program;</p>
          <p>(6) the degree of community stakeholder and institutional partner engagement;</p>
          <p>(7) the ability, or anticipated ability, of the school site and partners to continue to provide services in the absence of future funding under this section;</p>
          <p>(8) increases in access to services for students and their families; and</p>
          <p>(9) the degree of increased collaboration among participating agencies and private partners.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.231.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.44.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.25.34.0">2016 c 189 art 25 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.2.51.0">2023 c 55 art 2 s 51</a>, art 9 s 19</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.24"><b>124D.24</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.8.4.0">2014 c 272 art 8 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.25"><b>124D.25</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.8.4.0">2014 c 272 art 8 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.26"><b>124D.26</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.8.4.0">2014 c 272 art 8 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.27"><b>124D.27</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.8.4.0">2014 c 272 art 8 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.28"><b>124D.28</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.8.4.0">2014 c 272 art 8 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.29"><b>124D.29</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.8.4.0">2014 c 272 art 8 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.30"><b>124D.30</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.8.4.0">2014 c 272 art 8 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.31"><b>124D.31</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.8.4.0">2014 c 272 art 8 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.32"><b>124D.32</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.78.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 78</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.33"><b>124D.33</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/3/#laws.2.18.0">1Sp2001 c 3 art 2 s 18</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.331"><b>124D.331</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/3/#laws.2.18.0">1Sp2001 c 3 art 2 s 18</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">EMPLOYMENT-RELATED EDUCATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.34">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.34 MINNESOTA FOUNDATION FOR STUDENT ORGANIZATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Citation.</span></h2>
          <p>This section may be cited as the "Minnesota Foundation For Student Organizations Act."</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Creation of foundation.</span></h2>
          <p>There is created the Minnesota Foundation for Student Organizations. The purpose of the foundation is to promote career and technical student organizations and applied leadership opportunities in Minnesota public and nonpublic schools through public-private partnerships. The foundation is a nonprofit organization. The board of directors of the foundation and activities of the foundation are under the direction of the commissioner of education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Board of directors.</span></h2>
          <p>The board of directors of the Minnesota Foundation for Student Organizations consists of:</p>
          <p>(1) seven members appointed by the board of directors of the career and technical student organizations and chosen so that each represents one of the following career areas: agriculture, family and consumer sciences, service occupations, health occupations, marketing, business, and technical/industrial;</p>
          <p>(2) seven members from business, industry, and labor appointed by the governor to staggered terms and chosen so that each represents one of the following career areas: agriculture, family and consumer sciences, service occupations, health occupations, marketing, business, and technical/industrial;</p>
          <p>(3) five students or alumni of career and technical student organizations representing diverse career areas, three from secondary student organizations, and two from postsecondary student organizations. The students or alumni shall be appointed by the criteria and process agreed upon by the executive directors of the career and technical organizations; and</p>
          <p>(4) four members from education appointed by the governor to staggered terms and chosen so that each represents one of the following groups: school district level administrators, secondary school administrators, middle school administrators, and postsecondary administrators.</p>
          <p>Executive directors of career and technical education student organizations are ex officio, nonvoting members of the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Foundation programs.</span></h2>
          <p>The foundation shall advance applied leadership and intracurricular career and technical learning experiences for students. These may include, but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) recognition programs and awards for students demonstrating excellence in applied leadership;</p>
          <p>(2) summer programs for student leadership, career development, applied academics, and mentorship programs with business and industry;</p>
          <p>(3) recognition programs for teachers, administrators, and others who make outstanding contributions to career and technical programs;</p>
          <p>(4) outreach programs to increase the involvement of urban and suburban students;</p>
          <p>(5) organized challenges requiring cooperation and competition for secondary and postsecondary students;</p>
          <p>(6) assistance and training to community teams to increase career awareness and empowerment of youth as community leaders; and</p>
          <p>(7) assessment and activities in order to plan for and implement continuous improvement.</p>
          <p>To the extent possible, the foundation shall make these programs available to students in all parts of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Powers and duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The foundation may:</p>
          <p>(1) identify and plan common goals and priorities for the various career and technical student organizations in Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(2) publish brochures or booklets relating to the purposes of the foundation and collect reasonable fees for the publications;</p>
          <p>(3) seek and receive public and private money, grants, and in-kind services and goods from nonstate sources for the purposes of the foundation, without complying with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.013#stat.16A.013.1">16A.013, subdivision 1</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) contract with consultants on behalf of the career and technical student organizations;</p>
          <p>(5) plan, implement, and expend money for awards and other forms of recognition for career and technical student programs; and</p>
          <p>(6) identifying an appropriate name for the foundation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>The foundation board shall review and approve each contract of the board. Each contract of the foundation board shall be subject to the same review and approval procedures as a contract of the commissioner of education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Foundation staff.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner of education shall appoint the executive director of the foundation from three candidates nominated and submitted by the foundation board of directors and, as necessary, other staff who shall perform duties and have responsibilities solely related to the foundation. The employees appointed are not state employees under chapter 43A, but are covered under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.736">3.736</a>. The employees may participate in the state health and state insurance plans for employees in unclassified service. The employees shall be supervised by the executive director.</p>
          <p class="in">The commissioner shall appoint a liaison to the foundation board from the division in the department responsible for career and technical education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Public funding.</span></h2>
          <p>The state shall identify and secure appropriate funding for the basic staffing of the foundation and individual student career and technical student organizations at the state level.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Private funding.</span></h2>
          <p>The foundation must seek private resources to supplement the available public money. Individuals, businesses, and other organizations may contribute to the foundation in any manner specified by the board of directors. All money received shall be administered by the board of directors.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>The board of directors of the foundation must submit an annual report on the progress of its activities to the commissioner of education and to the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities. The annual report shall contain a financial report for the preceding year. The foundation shall submit a biennium report to the legislature before February 15, in the odd-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Appropriation.</span></h2>
          <p>All the amounts received by the foundation pursuant to this section are annually appropriated to the foundation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.34.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Student organizations.</span></h2>
          <p>Individual boards of career and technical education student organizations shall continue their operations in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.355">124D.355</a> and applicable federal law.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.34.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.4.3.0">1996 c 412 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.3.2.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 3 s 2</a>-9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.4.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 4</a>-8,103; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.3.4.0">1999 c 241 art 3 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.11.8.0">2007 c 146 art 11 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.2.12.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 2 s 12</a>-17</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.35">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.35 YOUTH ENTREPRENEURSHIP EDUCATION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall establish a youth entrepreneurship education program to improve the academic and entrepreneurial skills of students and aid in their transition from school to business creation. The program shall strengthen local economies by creating jobs that enable citizens to remain in their communities and to foster cooperation among educators, economic development professionals, business leaders, and representatives of labor. Assistance under this section shall be available to new or existing student-operated or school-operated businesses that have an educational purpose, and provide service or products for customers or clients who do not attend or work at the sponsoring school. The commissioner may require an equal local match for assistance under this section up to the maximum grant amount of $20,000.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/369/#laws.0.47.0">1993 c 369 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.1.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/369/#laws.0.8.0">1996 c 369 s 8</a>,12</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.355">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.355 VOCATIONAL EDUCATION STUDENT ORGANIZATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.355.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Activities of the organization.</span></h2>
          <p>Any student enrolled in a vocational technical education program approved by the commissioner of education or the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities may belong to a vocational student organization that is operated as an integral part of the vocational program. The commissioner of education and the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities may provide necessary technical assistance and leadership at the state level for administration of approved vocational student organizations and fiscal accounts, including administration of state and national conferences.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.355.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Accounts of the organization.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner and the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities may retain dues and other money collected on behalf of students participating in approved vocational student organizations and may deposit the money in separate accounts. The money in these accounts shall be available for expenditures for state and national activities related to specific organizations. Administration of money collected under this section is not subject to the provisions of chapters 15, 16A, 16B, and 16C, and may be deposited outside the state treasury. Money shall be administered under the policies of the applicable state board or agency relating to postsecondary and secondary vocational student organizations and is subject to audit by the legislative auditor. Any unexpended money shall not cancel but may be carried forward to the next fiscal year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.355.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/432/#laws.0.71.0">1975 c 432 s 71</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.3.4.0">1988 c 718 art 3 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.3.20.0">1989 c 329 art 3 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/375/#laws.0.3.0">1990 c 375 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.13.45.0">1993 c 224 art 13 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.13.25.0">1996 c 412 art 13 s 25</a>,26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/386/#laws.2.40.0">1998 c 386 art 2 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.36">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.36 CITATION; SERVEMINNESOTA INNOVATION ACT.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.37">124D.37</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.45">124D.45</a> shall be cited as the "ServeMinnesota Innovation Act."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.2.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.103.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 103</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.32.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.37">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.37 PURPOSE OF SERVEMINNESOTA INNOVATION ACT.</h1>
        <p>The purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.37">124D.37</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.45">124D.45</a> are to:</p>
        <p>(1) renew the ethic of civic responsibility in Minnesota;</p>
        <p>(2) empower youth to improve their life opportunities through literacy, job placement, and other essential skills;</p>
        <p>(3) empower government to meet its responsibility to prepare young people to be contributing members of society;</p>
        <p>(4) help meet human, educational, environmental, and public safety needs, particularly those needs relating to poverty;</p>
        <p>(5) prepare a citizenry that is academically competent, ready for work, and socially responsible;</p>
        <p>(6) demonstrate the connection between youth and community service, community service and education, and education and meaningful opportunities in the business community;</p>
        <p>(7) demonstrate the connection between providing opportunities for at-risk youth and reducing crime rates and the social costs of troubled youth;</p>
        <p>(8) create linkages for a comprehensive youth service and learning program in Minnesota including school age programs, higher education programs, youth work programs, and service corps programs; and</p>
        <p>(9) coordinate federal and state activities that advance the purposes in this section.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.3.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.103.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 103</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.33.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 33</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.38">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.38 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.38.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.37">124D.37</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.45">124D.45</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.38.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligible organization.</span></h2>
          <p>"Eligible organization" means:</p>
          <p>(1) a local unit of government including a statutory or home rule charter city, township, county, or group of two or more contiguous counties;</p>
          <p>(2) an existing nonprofit organization organized under chapter 317A;</p>
          <p>(3) an educational institution;</p>
          <p>(4) a private industry council;</p>
          <p>(5) a state agency; or</p>
          <p>(6) a federal agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.38.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Federal law.</span></h2>
          <p>"Federal law" means Public Law 111-13, as amended, or any other federal law or program assisting youth community service, work-based learning, or youth transition from school to work.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.38.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.51.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.38.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.51.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.38.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.51.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.38.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Program.</span></h2>
          <p>"Program" means an activity carried out with assistance provided under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.37">124D.37</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.45">124D.45</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.38.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Project.</span></h2>
          <p>"Project" means an activity that results in a specific identifiable service or product that could not be done from the resources of the eligible organization and that does not duplicate the routine services or functions of the eligible organization.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.38.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Commission.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commission" means the Minnesota commission on national and community service established in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.385">124D.385</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.38.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.51.0">2000 c 254 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.38.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.4.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.4.2.0">1994 c 647 art 4 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.2.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.103.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 103</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.20.0">2000 c 254 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.34.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.385">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.385 MINNESOTA COMMISSION ON NATIONAL AND COMMUNITY SERVICE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.385.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Commission on National and Community Service is established to assist the governor and the legislature in implementing sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.37">124D.37</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.45">124D.45</a> and federal law. Retroactive to the first Monday in January 1994, the terms of the members of the first commission shall be, as nearly as possible, one year for one-third of the members, two years for one-third of the members, and three years for one-third of the members. The members of the first commission shall determine the length of their terms by lot. Thereafter, the terms of commission members shall be for three years. Commission members may be reappointed upon the completion of their current term. The compensation, filling of vacancies, and removal of members are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575">15.0575</a>. The commission may accept gifts and contributions from public and private organizations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.385.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commission consists of 25 voting members. Voting members shall include the commissioner of education.</p>
          <p>(b) The governor shall appoint nine additional voting members that include a representative of organizations experienced in youth education, development and training, organizations promoting adult service and volunteerism, community-based service agencies or organizations, local public or private sector labor unions, local governments, business, a national service or service learning program, Indian tribes, and an individual between the ages of 16 and 25 who is a participant or supervisor in a program. The remaining 15 voting members appointed by the governor shall include individuals with expertise in the management and operations of a nonprofit organization, including individuals with expertise in public relations, finance, and development.</p>
          <p>(c) The governor shall appoint up to five ex officio nonvoting members from appropriate state agencies. A representative of the Corporation for National and Community Service shall also serve as an ex officio nonvoting member.</p>
          <p>(d) Voting and ex officio nonvoting members may appoint designees to act on their behalf. The number of voting members who are state employees shall not exceed 25 percent.</p>
          <p>(e) The governor shall ensure that, to the extent possible, the membership of the commission is balanced according to geography, race, ethnicity, age, and gender. The speaker of the house and the majority leader of the senate shall each appoint two legislators to be nonvoting members of the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.385.3">
