          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commission shall:</p>
          <p>(1) develop, with the assistance of the governor, the commissioner of education, and affected state agencies, a comprehensive state plan to provide services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.37">124D.37</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.45">124D.45</a> and federal law;</p>
          <p>(2) actively pursue public and private funding sources for services, including funding available under federal law;</p>
          <p>(3) administer the ServeMinnesota grant program under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.39">124D.39</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.44">124D.44</a>, including soliciting and approving grant applications from eligible organizations, and administering individual postservice benefits;</p>
          <p>(4) establish an evaluation plan for programs developed and services provided under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.37">124D.37</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.45">124D.45</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) report to the governor, commissioner of education, and legislature; and</p>
          <p>(6) administer the federal AmeriCorps Program.</p>
          <p>(b) Nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.37">124D.37</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.45">124D.45</a> precludes an organization from independently seeking public or private funding to accomplish purposes similar to those described in paragraph (a).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.385.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Delegation to nonprofit.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission may create a private nonprofit corporation that is exempt from taxation under section 501(c)(3) of the federal Internal Revenue Code of 1986. If the commission creates a private nonprofit corporation, the commission must serve as the corporation's board of directors. The private nonprofit corporation is not subject to laws governing state agencies or political subdivisions, except the provisions of chapter 13, the Open Meeting Law under chapter 13D, salary limits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15A.0815#stat.15A.0815.2">15A.0815, subdivision 2</a>, and audits by the legislative auditor under chapter 3 apply. Further provided that the board of directors and the executive director of the nonprofit corporation are each considered an "official" for purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/10A.071">10A.071</a>. The commission may delegate any or all of its powers and duties under federal law or under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.37">124D.37</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.45">124D.45</a> to the corporation if the nonprofit corporation is approved under federal law to administer the National and Community Service Trust Act. The commission may revoke a delegation of powers and duties at any time, and must revoke the delegation if the corporation is no longer approved under federal law as the administrator in the state of Minnesota for the National and Community Service Trust Act.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.385.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.5.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/483/#laws.0.1.0">1994 c 483 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.4.4.0">1994 c 647 art 4 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/131/#laws.0.2.0">1995 c 131 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.3.59.0">1995 c 212 art 3 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.30.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 30</a>,31; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.30.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.3.10.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 3 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.50.0">2000 c 254 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/220/#laws.10.32.0">2002 c 220 art 10 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/334/#laws.0.1.0">2002 c 334 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/286/#laws.0.1.0">2004 c 286 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.35.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 35</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.39">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.39 SERVEMINNESOTA INNOVATION PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>The ServeMinnesota Innovation program is established to provide funding for the commission to leverage federal and private funding to fulfill the purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.37">124D.37</a>. The ServeMinnesota Innovation program must supplement existing programs and services. The program must not displace existing programs and services, existing funding of programs or services, or existing employment and employment opportunities. No eligible organization may terminate, layoff, or reduce the hours of work of an employee to place or hire a program participant. No eligible organization may place or hire an individual for a project if an employee is on layoff from the same or a substantially equivalent position.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.39.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.6.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 6</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.3.20.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 3 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.9.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 9</a>,103; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.3.18.0">1998 c 398 art 3 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.36.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 36</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.40">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.40 SERVEMINNESOTA INNOVATION GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.40.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>An eligible organization interested in receiving a grant under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.39">124D.39</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.44">124D.44</a> may prepare and submit an application to the commission. As part of the grant application process, the commission must establish and publish grant application guidelines that are consistent with this subdivision, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.37">124D.37</a>, and Public Law 111-13; include criteria for reviewing an applicant's cost-benefit analysis; and require grantees to use research-based measures of program outcomes to generate valid and reliable data that are available to the commission for evaluation and public reporting purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.40.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grant authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission must use any state appropriation and any available federal funds, including any grant received under federal law, to award grants to establish programs for ServeMinnesota Innovation. At least one grant each must be available for a metropolitan proposal, a rural proposal, and a statewide proposal. If a portion of the suburban metropolitan area is not included in the metropolitan grant proposal, the statewide grant proposal must incorporate at least one suburban metropolitan area. In awarding grants, the commission may select at least one residential proposal and one nonresidential proposal.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.40.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.7.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 7</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.4.5.0">1994 c 647 art 4 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.3.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.3.20.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 3 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.10.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 10</a>,103; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.3.18.0">1998 c 398 art 3 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.21.0">2000 c 254 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.11.3.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.37.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 37</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.41"><b>124D.41</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/286/#laws.0.4.0">2004 c 286 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.42">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.42 READING AND MATH CORPS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.42.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/286/#laws.0.4.0">2004 c 286 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.42.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/286/#laws.0.4.0">2004 c 286 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.42.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/286/#laws.0.4.0">2004 c 286 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.42.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/286/#laws.0.4.0">2004 c 286 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.42.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/286/#laws.0.4.0">2004 c 286 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.42.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Program training.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission must, within available resources:</p>
          <p>(1) orient each grantee organization in the nature, philosophy, and purpose of the program;</p>
          <p>(2) build an ethic of community service through general community service training; and</p>
          <p>(3) provide guidance on integrating programmatic-based measurement into program models.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.42.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/286/#laws.0.4.0">2004 c 286 s 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.42.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Minnesota reading corps program.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A Minnesota reading corps program is established to provide ServeMinnesota AmeriCorps members with a data-based problem-solving model of literacy instruction to use in helping to train local Head Start program providers, other prekindergarten program providers, and staff in schools with students in kindergarten through grade 3 to evaluate and teach early literacy skills, including evidence-based literacy instruction under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.118">120B.118</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.124">120B.124</a>, to children age 3 to grade 3 and interventions for children in kindergarten to grade 3.</p>
          <p>(b) Literacy programs under this subdivision must comply with the provisions governing literacy program goals and data use under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.12#stat.142D.12.3">142D.12, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(c) Literacy programs under this subdivision must use evidence-based reading instruction and interventions focused on structured literacy. ServeMinnesota must demonstrate to the department that the training AmeriCorps members receive meets or exceeds the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.124#stat.120B.124.4">120B.124, subdivision 4</a>, for volunteers. Minnesota Reading Corps AmeriCorps members are not required to complete the training under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.24#stat.120B.24.4">120B.24, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) The commission must submit a biennial report to the committees of the legislature with jurisdiction over kindergarten through grade 12 education that records and evaluates program data to determine the efficacy of the programs under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.42.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Minnesota math corps program.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A Minnesota math corps program is established to give ServeMinnesota AmeriCorps members a data-based problem-solving model of mathematics instruction useful for providing elementary and middle school students and their teachers with instructional support to meet state academic standards in mathematics.</p>
          <p>(b) The commission must submit a biennial report to the committees of the legislature with jurisdiction over kindergarten through grade 12 education that records and evaluates program data to determine the efficacy of the programs under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.9.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 9</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.4.7.0">1994 c 647 art 4 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/131/#laws.0.2.0">1995 c 131 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.3.59.0">1995 c 212 art 3 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.5.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 5</a>-9,30; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.3.20.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 3 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.12.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 12</a>-16,103; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.3.18.0">1998 c 398 art 3 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.23.0">2000 c 254 s 23</a>-25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/133/#laws.3.1.0">2003 c 133 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.2.30.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 2 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/286/#laws.0.2.0">2004 c 286 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.2.45.0">2009 c 96 art 2 s 45</a>,46; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.38.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.3.24.0">2013 c 116 art 3 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.3.9.0">2023 c 55 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.26.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.4.18.0">2024 c 109 art 4 s 18</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.42.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.43"><b>124D.43</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/286/#laws.0.4.0">2004 c 286 s 4</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.44">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.44 MATCH REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <p>ServeMinnesota Innovation grant funds must be used for the living allowance, cost of employer taxes under sections 3111 and 3301 of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, workers' compensation coverage, health benefits, training and evaluation for each program participant, and administrative expenses, which must not exceed seven percent of total program costs. Applicant resources, from sources and in a form determined by the commission, must be used to provide for all other program costs.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.44.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.11.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 11</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.4.9.0">1994 c 647 art 4 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.11.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.3.20.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 3 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.103.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 103</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.3.18.0">1998 c 398 art 3 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.4.1.0">2000 c 489 art 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.39.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 39</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.45">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.45 EVALUATION AND REPORTING REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.45.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grantee organizations.</span></h2>
          <p>Each grantee organization shall report to the commission at the time and on the matters requested by the commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.45.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Interim report.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission must report annually to the legislature with interim recommendations to change the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.45.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.51.0">2000 c 254 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.45.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.12.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 12</a>,20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.4.10.0">1994 c 647 art 4 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.12.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.3.20.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 3 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.18.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 18</a>,19,103; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.3.18.0">1998 c 398 art 3 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.27.0">2000 c 254 s 27</a>,28; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.40.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.452">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.452 DISTRICT REPORT; CAREER AND TECHNICAL EDUCATION.</h1>
        <p>Each district and cooperative center must report data to the Department of Education for all career and technical education programs as required by the department.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.452.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.3.2.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 3 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.453"><b>124D.453</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.3.5.0">1999 c 241 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.4.9.0">2000 c 489 art 4 s 9</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.4531">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.4531 CAREER AND TECHNICAL REVENUE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4531.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Career and technical revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district with a career and technical program approved under this section for the fiscal year in which the levy is certified is eligible for career and technical revenue equal to 35 percent of approved expenditures in the fiscal year in which the levy is certified for the following:</p>
          <p>(1) salaries paid to essential, licensed personnel providing direct instructional services to students in that fiscal year, including extended contracts, for services rendered in the district's approved career and technical education programs, excluding salaries reimbursed by another school district under clause (2);</p>
          <p>(2) amounts paid to another Minnesota school district for salaries of essential, licensed personnel providing direct instructional services to students in that fiscal year for services rendered in the district's approved career and technical education programs;</p>
          <p>(3) contracted services provided by a public or private agency other than a Minnesota school district or cooperative center under chapter 123A or 136D;</p>
          <p>(4) necessary travel between instructional sites by licensed career and technical education personnel;</p>
          <p>(5) necessary travel by licensed career and technical education personnel for vocational student organization activities held within the state for instructional purposes;</p>
          <p>(6) curriculum development activities that are part of a five-year plan for improvement based on program assessment;</p>
          <p>(7) necessary travel by licensed career and technical education personnel for noncollegiate credit-bearing professional development; and</p>
          <p>(8) specialized vocational instructional supplies.</p>
          <p>(b) The district must recognize the full amount of this levy as revenue for the fiscal year in which it is certified.</p>
          <p>(c) The amount of the revenue calculated under this subdivision may not exceed $17,850,000 for taxes payable in 2012, $15,520,000 for taxes payable in 2013, and $20,657,000 for taxes payable in 2014.</p>
          <p>(d) If the estimated revenue exceeds the amount in paragraph (c), the commissioner must reduce the percentage in paragraph (a) until the estimated revenue no longer exceeds the limit in paragraph (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4531.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Career and technical levy.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For fiscal year 2014 only, a district may levy an amount not more than the product of its career and technical revenue times the lesser of one or the ratio of its adjusted net tax capacity per adjusted pupil unit in the fiscal year in which the levy is certified to the career and technical revenue equalizing factor. The career and technical revenue equalizing factor for fiscal year 2014 equals $7,612.</p>
          <p>(b) For fiscal year 2015 and later, a district may levy an amount not more than the product of its career and technical revenue times the lesser of one or the ratio of its adjusted net tax capacity per adjusted pupil unit in the fiscal year in which the levy is certified to the career and technical revenue equalizing factor. The career and technical revenue equalizing factor for fiscal year 2015 and later equals $7,612.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4531.1b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1b.<span class="headnote">Career and technical aid.</span></h2>
          <p>For fiscal year 2014 and later, a district's career and technical aid equals its career and technical revenue less its career and technical levy. If the district levy is less than the permitted levy, the district's career and technical aid shall be reduced proportionately.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4531.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Allocation from cooperative centers and intermediate districts.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, a cooperative center or an intermediate district must allocate its approved expenditures for career and technical education programs among participating districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4531.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Revenue guarantee.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 1, paragraph (a), the career and technical education revenue for a district is not less than the lesser of:</p>
          <p>(1) the district's career and technical education revenue for the previous fiscal year; or</p>
          <p>(2) 100 percent of the approved expenditures for career and technical programs included in subdivision 1, paragraph (a), for the fiscal year in which the levy is certified.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4531.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Revenue adjustments.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivisions 1, 1a, and 3, for taxes payable in 2012 to 2014 only, the department must calculate the career and technical revenue for each district according to Minnesota Statutes 2010, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.4531">124D.4531</a>, and adjust the revenue for each district proportionately to meet the statewide revenue target under subdivision 1, paragraph (c). For purposes of calculating the revenue guarantee under subdivision 3, the career and technical education revenue for the previous fiscal year is the revenue according to Minnesota Statutes 2010, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.4531">124D.4531</a>, before adjustments to meet the statewide revenue target.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4531.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">District reports.</span></h2>
          <p>Each district or cooperative center must report data to the department for all career and technical education programs as required by the department to implement the career and technical revenue formula.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4531.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Allocation from districts participating in agreements for secondary education or interdistrict cooperation.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, a district with a career and technical program approved under this section that participates in an agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.30">123A.30</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.32">123A.32</a> must allocate its revenue authority under this section among participating districts.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.4531.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.2.66.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 2 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/88/#laws.2.1.0">2009 c 88 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.41.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/239/#laws.1.21.0">2012 c 239 art 1 s 21</a>-23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.1.14.0">2013 c 116 art 1 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/144/#laws.0.6.0">2013 c 144 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.3.43.0">2014 c 272 art 3 s 43</a>-45</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.4535">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.4535 INNOVATIVE DELIVERY OF CAREER AND TECHNICAL EDUCATION PROGRAMS; SHARING OF DISTRICT RESOURCES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4535.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment; requirements for participation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A program is established to improve student, career and college readiness, and school outcomes by allowing groups of school districts to work together in partnership with local and regional postsecondary institutions and programs, community institutions, and other private, public, for-profit, and nonprofit workplace partners, to:</p>
          <p>(1) provide innovative education programs and activities that integrate core academic and career and technical subjects in students' programs of study through coordinated secondary and postsecondary career and technical programs leading to an industry certification or other credential;</p>
          <p>(2) provide embedded professional development for program participants;</p>
          <p>(3) use performance assessments in authentic settings to measure students' technical skills and progress toward attaining an industry certification or other credential; and</p>
          <p>(4) efficiently share district, institution, and workplace resources.</p>
          <p>(b) To participate in this program to improve student, career and college readiness, and school outcomes, a group of two or more school districts must collaborate with school staff and project partners and receive formal school board approval to form a partnership. The partnership must develop a plan to provide challenging programmatic options for students under paragraph (a); create professional development opportunities for educators and other program participants; increase student engagement and connection and challenging learning opportunities for diverse populations of students that are focused on employability skills and technical, job-specific skills related to a specific career pathway; or demonstrate efficiencies in delivering financial and other services needed to realize plan goals and objectives. The plan must include:</p>
          <p>(1) collaborative education goals and objectives;</p>
          <p>(2) strategies and processes to implement those goals and objectives, including a budget process with periodic expenditure reviews;</p>
          <p>(3) valid and reliable measures including performance assessments in authentic settings and progress toward attaining an industry certification or other credential, among other measures, to evaluate progress in realizing plan goals and objectives;</p>
          <p>(4) an implementation timeline; and</p>
          <p>(5) other applicable conditions, regulations, responsibilities, duties, provisions, fee schedules, and legal considerations needed to fully implement the plan.</p>
          <p class="bl">A partnership may invite additional districts or other participants under paragraph (a) to join the partnership after notifying the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) A partnership of interested districts must submit an application to the commissioner of education in the form and manner the commissioner determines, consistent with the requirements of this section. The application must contain the formal approval adopted by the school board in each district to participate in the plan.</p>
          <p>(d) Notwithstanding any other law to the contrary, a participating school district under this section continues to: receive revenue and maintain its taxation authority; be organized and governed by an elected school board with general powers under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.02">123B.02</a>; and be subject to employment agreements under chapter 122A and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/179A.20">179A.20</a>; and district employees continue to remain employees of the employing school district.</p>
          <p>(e) Participating districts must submit a biennial report by February 1 in each odd-numbered year to the education committees of the legislature and the commissioner of education that includes performance assessment, high school graduation, and career and technical certification data to show the success of the partnership in preparing diverse populations of students for careers and jobs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4535.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's role.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of education must convene an advisory panel to advise the commissioner on applicants' qualifications to participate in this program. The commissioner must ensure an equitable geographical distribution of program participants to the extent practicable. The commissioner must select only those applicants that fully comply with subdivision 1. The commissioner may terminate a program participant that fails to effectively implement the goals and objectives contained in its application and according to its stated timeline.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.4535.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.2.35.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 2 s 35</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.4536">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.4536 CAREER AND TECHNICAL EDUCATION CONSORTIUM GRANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4536.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>"Career and technical education (CTE) consortium" means a voluntary collaboration of the Minnesota Service Cooperatives and other regional public and private partners, including school districts, intermediate school districts, vocational cooperatives, and higher education institutions, that work together to provide career and technical education opportunities for students.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4536.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A CTE consortium must:</p>
          <p>(1) develop career pathways for students;</p>
          <p>(2) develop new career and technical programs that focus on the industry sectors that fuel the regional economy;</p>
          <p>(3) facilitate the development of highly trained and knowledgeable students who are equipped with technical and workplace skills needed by regional employers;</p>
          <p>(4) improve access to career and technical education programs for students by developing public and private partnerships with labor, business, and industry leaders and by increasing coordination of high school and postsecondary program options;</p>
          <p>(5) increase family and student awareness of the availability and benefit of career and technical education courses and training opportunities; and</p>
          <p>(6) provide industry-level equipment and technologies supporting skill development as identified by CTE consortia partners.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the requirements in paragraph (a), a CTE consortium may:</p>
          <p>(1) address the teacher shortage crisis in career and technical education through incentive funding and training programs;</p>
          <p>(2) provide professional development for training teachers in curriculum and skill development in focus areas identified by CTE consortia partners; and</p>
          <p>(3) provide transportation reimbursement grants to provide equitable opportunities throughout the region for students to participate in career and technical education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4536.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Career and technical education advisory committee.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Service Cooperatives must establish a career and technical education advisory committee to provide advice on the administration of a CTE consortium.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4536.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Private funding.</span></h2>
          <p>A CTE consortium may receive other sources of funds to supplement state funding. All funds received must be administered by the Minnesota Service Cooperatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4536.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Reporting requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 15 of each year, a CTE consortium receiving funding under this section must submit an annual report on the progress of its activities to the commissioner of education and the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over secondary and postsecondary education. The annual report must contain a financial report for the preceding fiscal year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.4536.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Grant awards.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Service Cooperatives serves as the fiscal host for grants awarded under this section. The Minnesota Service Cooperatives may consult with the commissioner to award grants to any CTE consortium that qualifies under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.4536.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.1.6.0">2023 c 55 art 1 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.454">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.454 ACCESS TO MINNESOTA'S TRANSITION SYSTEM FOR CHILDREN WITH A DISABILITY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of this section is to provide a method to fund career and technical education programs for children with a disability that are components of the student's transition plan. As used in this section, the term "children with a disability" shall have the meaning ascribed to it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.02">125A.02</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">For the purposes of this section, the definitions in this subdivision apply.</p>
          <p class="in">(a) "Basic revenue" has the meaning given it in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>. For the purposes of computing basic revenue pursuant to this section, each child with a disability shall be counted as prescribed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.1">126C.05, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) "Essential personnel" means a licensed teacher, licensed support services staff person, paraprofessional providing direct services to students, or licensed personnel under subdivision 12. This definition is not intended to change or modify the definition of essential employee in chapter 179A.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.32.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.3.25.0">2007 c 146 art 3 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.3.25.0">2007 c 146 art 3 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.3.25.0">2007 c 146 art 3 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.3.25.0">2007 c 146 art 3 s 25</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Use of aid.</span></h2>
          <p>The aid provided under this section shall be paid only for services rendered or for the costs which are incurred according to this section for transition programs for children with a disability which are approved by the commissioner of education and operated in accordance with rules promulgated by the commissioner. These rules shall be subject to the restrictions provided in subdivision 12. The procedure for application for approval of these programs shall be as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.75">125A.75</a>, subdivisions 4 and 6, and the application review process shall be conducted by the Division of Federal Programs in the department.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Payment of aid.</span></h2>
          <p>All aid pursuant to this section shall be paid to the district or cooperative center providing the services. All aid received by a district or center from any source for transition programs for children with a disability shall be utilized solely for that purpose.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.32.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.5.32.0">2013 c 116 art 5 s 32</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.454.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Compliance with rules.</span></h2>
          <p>Aid must be paid under this section only for services rendered or for costs incurred in career and technical education programs approved by the commissioner and operated in accordance with rules promulgated by the commissioner. This aid shall be paid only for services rendered and for costs incurred by essential, licensed personnel who meet the requirements for licensure pursuant to the rules of the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board. Licensed personnel means persons holding a valid career and technical license issued by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.15">122A.15</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.33">122A.33</a>. If an average of five or fewer secondary full-time equivalent students are enrolled per teacher in an approved postsecondary program at Intermediate District No. 287, 916, or 917, licensed personnel means persons holding a valid vocational license issued by the commissioner or the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.42">127A.42</a>, the commissioner may modify or withdraw the program or aid approval and withhold aid under this section without proceeding under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.42">127A.42</a> at any time. To do so, the commissioner must determine that the program does not comply with rules of the Department of Education or that any facts concerning the program or its budget differ from the facts in the district's approved application.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.454.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/764/#laws.0.72.0">1978 c 764 s 72</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/334/#laws.5.28.0">1979 c 334 art 5 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.5.43.0">1981 c 358 art 5 s 43</a>-45; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/548/#laws.5.16.0">1982 c 548 art 5 s 16</a>-18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/12/#laws.3.18.0">1Sp1985 c 12 art 3 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.3.23.0">1987 c 398 art 3 s 23</a>-25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/1/4/#laws.1.1.0">1Sp1987 c 4 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.3.12.0">1989 c 329 art 3 s 12</a>-14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.15.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 15</a>,38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.3.26.0">1993 c 224 art 3 s 26</a>-28; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/374/#laws.0.9.0">1993 c 374 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.13.5.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 13 s 5</a>; art 15 s 18-24; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.28.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 28</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.3.3.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 3 s 3</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/294/#laws.1.6.0">2004 c 294 art 1 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.3.6.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 3 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.3.2.0">2007 c 146 art 3 s 2</a>,3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.26.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/55/#laws.1.32.0">2022 c 55 art 1 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.46">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.46 EDUCATION AND EMPLOYMENT TRANSITIONS SYSTEM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.46.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Goals.</span></h2>
          <p>To better prepare all learners to make transitions between education and employment, a comprehensive education and employment transitions system is established that is driven by multisector partnerships and takes a lifelong approach to workforce development. The goals of the statewide education and employment transitions system are:</p>
          <p>(1) to improve the skills learners need to achieve greater levels of self-sufficiency through education, training, and work;</p>
          <p>(2) to improve work-related counseling and information about career opportunities and vocational education programs available to learners to facilitate workforce development;</p>
          <p>(3) to integrate opportunities for work-based learning, service-learning, and other applied learning methods into the elementary, secondary, and postsecondary curriculum and state and local graduation standards;</p>
          <p>(4) to increase participation in employment opportunities and demonstrate the relationship between education and employment at the elementary, secondary, and postsecondary education levels;</p>
          <p>(5) to promote the efficient use of public and private resources by coordinating elementary, secondary, and postsecondary education with related government programs;</p>
          <p>(6) to expand educational options available to all learners through collaborative efforts between school districts, postsecondary institutions, employers, organized labor, workers, learners, parents, community-based organizations, and other interested parties;</p>
          <p>(7) to increase opportunities for women, minorities, individuals with a disability, and at-risk learners to fully participate in work-based learning;</p>
          <p>(8) to establish performance standards for learners that integrate state and local graduation standards and generally recognized industry and occupational skill standards; and</p>
          <p>(9) to provide support systems including a unified labor market information system; a centralized quality assurance system with information on learner achievement, employer satisfaction, and measurable system outcomes; a statewide marketing system to promote the importance of lifework development; a comprehensive professional development system for public and private sector partners; and a comprehensive system for providing technical support to local partnerships for education and employment transitions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.46.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Funding.</span></h2>
          <p>Work-based learning programs incorporating postsecondary instruction implemented under this section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.47">124D.47</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.49">124D.49</a> shall provide for student funding according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.09">124D.09</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.46.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Governor's Workforce Development Board.</span></h2>
          <p>The governor's Workforce Development Board is responsible for developing, implementing, and evaluating the statewide education and employment transitions system and achieving the goals of the system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.46.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Partnership grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The board must award grants to implement local education and employment transition partnerships established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.49">124D.49</a>. Grants under this section may be used for the local education and employment transitions system, youth apprenticeship and other work-based learning programs, youth employer programs, youth entrepreneurship programs, and other programs and purposes the board determines fulfill the purposes of the education and employment transitions system. The board must evaluate grant proposals on the basis of the elements required in the local plan described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.49#stat.124D.49.3">124D.49, subdivision 3</a>. The board must develop and publicize the grant application process and review and comment on the proposals submitted. Priority in awarding grants must be given to local partnerships that include multiple communities and a viable base of educational, work-based learning, and employment opportunities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.46.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Expired]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.46.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Expired]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.46.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/335/#laws.0.1.0">1993 c 335 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.23.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.99.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 99</a>,100,103; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.1.108.0">2018 c 182 art 1 s 108</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.47">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.47 COMPREHENSIVE YOUTH APPRENTICESHIP PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.47.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Academic instruction and work-related learning.</span></h2>
          <p>Comprehensive youth apprenticeship programs and other work-based learning programs under the education and employment transitions system must integrate academic instruction and work-related learning in the classroom and at the workplace. Schools, in collaboration with learners' employers, must use competency-based measures to evaluate learners' progress in the program. Learners who successfully complete the program must receive academic and occupational credentials from the participating school.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.47.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Youth apprenticeship programs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A comprehensive youth apprenticeship program must require representatives of secondary and postsecondary school systems, affected local businesses, industries, occupations and labor, as well as the local community, to be actively and collaboratively involved in advising and managing the program and ensuring, in consultation with local private industry councils, that the youth apprenticeship program meets local labor market demands, provides student apprentices with the high skill training necessary for career advancement, meets applicable state graduation requirements and labor standards, pays apprentices for their work and provides support services to program participants.</p>
          <p>(b) Local employers, collaborating with labor organizations where appropriate, must assist the program by analyzing workplace needs, creating work-related curriculum, employing and adequately paying youth apprentices engaged in work-related learning in the workplace, training youth apprentices to become skilled in an occupation, providing student apprentices with a workplace mentor, periodically informing the school of an apprentice's progress, and making a reasonable effort to employ youth apprentices who successfully complete the program.</p>
          <p>(c) A student participating in a comprehensive youth apprenticeship program must sign a youth apprenticeship agreement with participating entities that obligates youth apprentices, their parents or guardians, employers, and schools to meet program requirements; indicates how academic instruction, work-based learning, and worksite learning and experience will be integrated; ensures that successful youth apprentices will receive a recognized credential of academic and occupational proficiency; and establishes the wage rate and other benefits for which youth apprentices are eligible while employed during the program.</p>
          <p>(d) Secondary school principals, counselors, or business mentors familiar with the education to employment transitions system must inform entering secondary school students about available occupational and career opportunities and the option of entering a youth apprenticeship or other work-based learning program to obtain postsecondary academic and occupational credentials.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.47.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.14.17.0">1993 c 224 art 14 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/335/#laws.0.3.0">1993 c 335 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/374/#laws.0.25.0">1993 c 374 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.24.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 24</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.103.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 103</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.475">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.475 CREDIT FOR EMPLOYMENT WITH HEALTH CARE PROVIDERS.</h1>
        <p>Consistent with the career and technical pathways program, a student in grade 11 or 12 who is employed by an institutional long-term care or licensed assisted living facility, a home and community-based services and supports provider, a hospital or health system clinic, or a child care center may earn up to two elective credits each year toward graduation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.024#stat.120B.024.1">120B.024, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (a), clause (7), at the discretion of the enrolling school district or charter school. A student may earn one elective credit for every 350 hours worked, including hours worked during the summer. A student who is employed by an eligible employer must submit an application, in the form or manner required by the school district or charter school, for elective credit to the school district or charter school in order to receive elective credit. The school district or charter school must verify the hours worked with the employer before awarding elective credit.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.475.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.2.52.0">2023 c 55 art 2 s 52</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.48">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.48 GENERAL APPLICATION OF WORKPLACE HEALTH AND SAFETY LAWS; DISPLACEMENT OF WORKERS PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <p>(a) All state and federal laws relating to workplace health and safety apply to youth apprenticeships.</p>
        <p>(b) The employment of a youth apprentice may not displace or cause any reduction in the number of nonovertime hours worked, wages, or benefits of a currently employed worker.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.48.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/335/#laws.0.6.0">1993 c 335 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.103.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 103</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.49">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.49 EDUCATION AND EMPLOYMENT TRANSITIONS PARTNERSHIPS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.49.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Local partnerships; establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>Local education and employment transitions partnerships may be established to implement local education and employment transitions systems. Local partnerships must represent multiple sectors in the community, including, at a minimum, representatives of employers, primary and secondary education, labor and professional organizations, workers, learners, parents, community-based organizations, and to the extent possible, postsecondary education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.49.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Board.</span></h2>
          <p>A local education and employment transitions partnership must establish a governing board for planning and implementing work-based and other applied learning programs. The board must consist of at least one representative from each member of the education and employment transitions partnership. A majority of the board must consist of representatives of local or regional employers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.49.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Local education and employment transitions systems.</span></h2>
          <p>A local education and employment transitions partnership must assess the needs of employers, employees, and learners, and develop a plan for implementing and achieving the objectives of a local or regional education and employment transitions system. The plan must provide for a comprehensive local system for assisting learners and workers in making the transition from school to work or for retraining in a new vocational area. The objectives of a local education and employment transitions system include:</p>
          <p>(1) increasing the effectiveness of the educational programs and curriculum of elementary, secondary, and postsecondary schools and the work site in preparing students in the skills and knowledge needed to be successful in the workplace;</p>
          <p>(2) implementing learner outcomes for students in grades kindergarten through 12 designed to introduce the world of work and to explore career opportunities, including nontraditional career opportunities;</p>
          <p>(3) eliminating barriers to providing effective integrated applied learning, service-learning, or work-based curriculum;</p>
          <p>(4) increasing opportunities to apply academic knowledge and skills, including skills needed in the workplace, in local settings which include the school, school-based enterprises, postsecondary institutions, the workplace, and the community;</p>
          <p>(5) increasing applied instruction in the attitudes and skills essential for success in the workplace, including cooperative working, leadership, problem-solving, English language proficiency, and respect for diversity;</p>
          <p>(6) providing staff training for vocational guidance counselors, teachers, and other appropriate staff in the importance of preparing learners for the transition to work, and in methods of providing instruction that incorporate applied learning, work-based learning, English language proficiency, and service-learning experiences;</p>
          <p>(7) identifying and enlisting local and regional employers who can effectively provide work-based or service-learning opportunities, including, but not limited to, apprenticeships, internships, and mentorships;</p>
          <p>(8) recruiting community and workplace mentors including peers, parents, employers and employed individuals from the community, and employers of high school students;</p>
          <p>(9) identifying current and emerging educational, training, native and English language development, and employment needs of the area or region, especially within industries with potential for job growth;</p>
          <p>(10) improving the coordination and effectiveness of local vocational and job training programs, including vocational education, adult basic education, tech prep, apprenticeship, service-learning, youth entrepreneur, youth training and employment programs administered by the commissioner of employment and economic development, and local job training programs under the Workforce Investment Act of 1998, Public Law 105-220;</p>
          <p>(11) identifying and applying for federal, state, local, and private sources of funding for vocational or applied learning programs;</p>
          <p>(12) providing students with current information and counseling about career opportunities, potential employment, educational opportunities in postsecondary institutions, workplaces, and the community, and the skills and knowledge necessary to succeed;</p>
          <p>(13) providing educational technology, including interactive television networks and other distance learning methods, to ensure access to a broad variety of work-based learning opportunities;</p>
          <p>(14) including students with disabilities in a district's vocational or applied learning program and ways to serve at-risk learners through collaboration with area learning centers under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.05">123A.05</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.09">123A.09</a>, or other alternative programs; and</p>
          <p>(15) providing a warranty to employers, postsecondary education programs, and other postsecondary training programs, that learners successfully completing a high school work-based or applied learning program will be able to apply the knowledge and work skills included in the program outcomes or graduation requirements. The warranty shall require education and training programs to continue to work with those learners that need additional skill or English language development until they can demonstrate achievement of the program outcomes or graduation requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.49.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Annual reports.</span></h2>
          <p>A local education and employment transitions partnership must annually publish a report and submit information to the council as required. The report must include information required by the council for the statewide system performance assessment. The report must be available to the public in the communities served by the local education and employment transitions partnership. The report must be published no later than September 1 of the year following the year in which the data was collected.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.49.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.26.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 26</a>,30; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.101.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 101</a>,103; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.31.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.1.33.0">2014 c 272 art 1 s 33</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.50">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.50 SERVICE-LEARNING AND WORK-BASED LEARNING CURRICULUM AND PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.50.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Service-learning and work-based learning programs study.</span></h2>
          <p>The governor's Workforce Development Board must assist the commissioner in studying how to combine community service activities and service-learning with work-based learning programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.50.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Service-learning programs developed.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner, in consultation with the commission, shall develop a service-learning program curriculum that includes a policy framework and strategies for youth community service and an infrastructure for mentoring youth. The commissioner shall include in the curriculum at least the following:</p>
          <p>(1) youth community service strategies that enable young people to make significant contributions to the welfare of their community through such organizations as schools, colleges, government agencies, and community-based organizations or through individual efforts;</p>
          <p>(2) mentoring strategies that enable young people to be matched with caring, responsible individuals who can encourage and guide the young people in their personal growth and development;</p>
          <p>(3) guidelines, criteria, and procedures for community service programs that incorporate the results of the study in subdivision 1; and</p>
          <p>(4) criteria for community service activities and service-learning.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.50.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Structuring programs according to grade or education level.</span></h2>
          <p>The service-learning curriculum must accommodate students' grade level or the last completed grade level of the participants not currently enrolled in school. Schools must provide at least the following:</p>
          <p>(1) for students in grades 7 to 9, an opportunity to learn about service-learning activities and possible occupations;</p>
          <p>(2) for students in grade 10, an opportunity to apply for service-learning under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.19">124D.19 subdivision 10</a>, and youth apprenticeship programs; and</p>
          <p>(3) for students in grades 11 and 12 and young people not currently enrolled in school, an opportunity to become involved in community service activities, participate in youth apprenticeship programs, and, depending upon the individual's demonstrated abilities, complete high school or pursue postsecondary coursework.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.50.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Programs following youth community service.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Minnesota Commission on National and Community Service in cooperation with the governor's Workforce Development Board, the commissioner and the Minnesota Office of Higher Education, shall provide for those participants who successfully complete youth community service under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.39">124D.39</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.44">124D.44</a>, the following:</p>
          <p>(1) for those who have a high school diploma or its equivalent, an opportunity to participate in a youth apprenticeship program at a community or technical college; and</p>
          <p>(2) for those who are postsecondary students, an opportunity to participate in an educational program that supplements postsecondary courses leading to a degree or a statewide credential of academic and occupational proficiency.</p>
          <p>(b) The governor's Workforce Development Board, in cooperation with the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities, must establish a mechanism to transfer credit earned in a youth apprenticeship program between the technical colleges and other postsecondary institutions offering applied associate degrees.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.50.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.14.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.4.13.0">1994 c 647 art 4 s 13</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/212/#laws.3.59.0">1995 c 212 art 3 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 13</a>,14,30; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.51.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 51</a>,52,103; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/107/#laws.2.60.0">2005 c 107 art 2 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.43.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.1.108.0">2018 c 182 art 1 s 108</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.505">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.505 MINNESOTA CAREER INFORMATION SYSTEM.</h1>
        <p>(a) The Department of Education, through the Minnesota career information system (MCIS), may provide career information to, including, but not limited to, school districts and other educational organizations, employment and training services, human service agencies, libraries, and families. The department shall collect fees necessary to recover all expenditures related to the operation of the Minnesota career information system.</p>
        <p>(b) Grants and other legislative funds may be accepted and used for the improvement or operation of the Minnesota career information system. All receipts shall be deposited in a special account in the special revenue fund that shall be carried over at the end of each fiscal year. The money in the account, along with any interest earned, is appropriated to the commissioner of education for the Minnesota career information system and must be for the sole use and benefit of the system. The department shall recognize that the Minnesota career information system operates under a self-supporting directive, and accordingly, must provide sufficient administrative latitude within the confines of law to enable the system to operate effectively.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.505.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.3.1.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.4.51.0">1998 c 397 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ADULT EDUCATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.51">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.51 EVENING SCHOOLS; ADULT AND CONTINUING EDUCATION.</h1>
        <p>The board may establish and maintain public evening schools and adult and continuing education programs. The evening schools and adult and continuing education programs maintained by the board must be available to all persons over 16 years of age who, from any cause, are unable to attend the full-time elementary or secondary schools of such district.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.51.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
