          <p>"Approved recovery program" means a course of instruction offered by a recovery school that provides academic services, assistance with recovery, and continuing care to students recovering from substance abuse or dependency. A recovery program may be offered in a transitional academic setting designed to meet graduation requirements. A recovery program must be approved by the commissioner of education. The commissioner may specify the manner and form of the application for the approval of a recovery school or recovery program. The commissioner must also approve any unreimbursed pupil transportation costs incurred by students participating in an approved recovery program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.695.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An approved recovery program is eligible for an annual recovery program grant of up to $125,000 to pay for a portion of the costs under this section for recovery program support staff and approved pupil transportation expenses.</p>
          <p>(b) "Recovery program support staff" means licensed alcohol and substance use disorder counselors, licensed school counselors, licensed school psychologists, licensed school nurses, and licensed school social workers.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.695.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.15.7.0">2014 c 312 art 15 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.2.37.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 2 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.70"><b>124D.70</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.2.62.0">1999 c 241 art 2 s 62</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">AMERICAN INDIAN EDUCATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.71">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.71 CITATION; AMERICAN INDIAN EDUCATION ACT.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a> may be cited as the "American Indian Education Act of 1988."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.71.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/312/#laws.0.1.0">1977 c 312 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.3.5.0">1988 c 718 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.164.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.72">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.72 POLICY.</h1>
        <p>The legislature finds that a more adequate education is needed for American Indian people in the state of Minnesota. The legislature recognizes the unique educational and culturally related academic needs of American Indian people. The legislature also is concerned about the lack of American Indian teachers in the state. Therefore, pursuant to the policy of the state to ensure equal educational opportunity to every individual, it is the purpose of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a> to provide for American Indian education programs specially designed to meet these unique educational or culturally related academic needs or both.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.72.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/312/#laws.0.2.0">1977 c 312 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/706/#laws.0.43.0">1978 c 706 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.3.6.0">1988 c 718 art 3 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.164.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.73">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.73 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.73.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a>, the terms defined in this section have the meanings given them.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.73.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.1.20.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 1 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.73.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Tribal Nations Education Committee.</span></h2>
          <p>"Tribal Nations Education Committee" means the committee established through tribal directive that the commissioner consults with on American Indian education programs, policy, and all matters related to educating Minnesota's American Indian students.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.73.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Participating school; American Indian school.</span></h2>
          <p>"Participating school" and "American Indian school" mean a school that:</p>
          <p>(1) is not operated by a school district; and</p>
          <p>(2) is eligible for a grant under federal Title VI of the Elementary and Secondary Education Act for the education of American Indian children.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.73.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">American Indian student.</span></h2>
          <p>"American Indian student" means a student who identifies as American Indian or Alaska Native, as defined by the state on October 1 of the previous school year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.73.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/312/#laws.0.3.0">1977 c 312 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/219/#laws.0.1.0">1979 c 219 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.3.7.0">1988 c 718 art 3 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.164.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.45.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 45</a>,46; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.25.36.0">2016 c 189 art 25 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.4.8.0">2023 c 55 art 4 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.74">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.74 AMERICAN INDIAN EDUCATION PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.74.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program described.</span></h2>
          <p>American Indian education programs are programs in public elementary and secondary schools, nonsectarian nonpublic, community, Tribal, charter, or alternative schools enrolling American Indian children designed to:</p>
          <p>(1) support postsecondary preparation for American Indian pupils;</p>
          <p>(2) support the academic achievement of American Indian pupils;</p>
          <p>(3) make the curriculum relevant to the needs, interests, and cultural heritage of American Indian pupils;</p>
          <p>(4) provide positive reinforcement of the self-image of American Indian pupils;</p>
          <p>(5) develop intercultural awareness among pupils, parents, and staff; and</p>
          <p>(6) supplement, not supplant, state and federal educational and cocurricular programs.</p>
          <p class="bl">Program services designed to increase completion and graduation rates of American Indian students must emphasize academic achievement, retention, and attendance; development of support services for staff, including in-service training and technical assistance in methods of teaching American Indian pupils; research projects, including innovative teaching approaches and evaluation of methods of relating to American Indian pupils; provision of career counseling to American Indian pupils; modification of curriculum, instructional methods, and administrative procedures to meet the needs of American Indian pupils; and instruction in American Indian language, literature, history, and culture. Districts offering programs may make contracts for the provision of program services by establishing cooperative liaisons with Tribal programs and American Indian social service agencies. These programs may also be provided as components of early childhood and family education programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.74.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Voluntary enrollment.</span></h2>
          <p>Enrollment in American Indian education programs must be voluntary. School districts and participating schools must make affirmative efforts to encourage participation. They shall encourage parents to visit classes or come to school for a conference explaining the nature of the program and provide visits by school staff to parents' homes to explain the nature of the program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.74.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Enrollment of other children; shared time enrollment.</span></h2>
          <p>To the extent that the unique educational and culturally related academic needs of American Indian people are met and American Indian student accountability factors are the same or higher than their non-American Indian peers, a district or participating school may make provision for the voluntary enrollment of non-American Indian children in the instructional components of an American Indian education program in order that they may acquire an understanding of the cultural heritage of the American Indian children for whom that particular program is designed. However, in determining eligibility to participate in a program, priority must be given to American Indian children. American Indian children enrolled in an existing nonpublic school system may be enrolled on a shared time basis in American Indian education programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.74.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Location of programs.</span></h2>
          <p>American Indian education programs must be located in educational settings in which regular classes in a variety of subjects are offered on a daily basis. Programs may operate on an extended day or extended year basis, including school districts, charter schools, and Tribal contract schools that offer virtual learning environments.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.74.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Assignment of students.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district or participating school must not, in providing these programs, assign students to schools in a way which will have the effect of promoting segregation of students by race, sex, color, or national origin.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.74.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Nonverbal courses and extracurricular activities.</span></h2>
          <p>In predominantly nonverbal subjects, such as art, music, and physical education, American Indian children shall participate fully and on an equal basis with their peers in school classes provided for these subjects. Every school district or participating school shall ensure to children enrolled in American Indian education programs an equal and meaningful opportunity to participate fully with other children in all extracurricular activities. This subdivision shall not be construed to prohibit instruction in nonverbal subjects or extracurricular activities which relate to the cultural heritage of the American Indian children, or which are otherwise necessary to accomplish the objectives described in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.74.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">American Indian culture and language classes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district or participating school that conducts American Indian education programs under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a> must provide American Indian culture and language classes if: (1) at least five percent of students are American Indian students; or (2) 100 or more students are American Indian students.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this subdivision, "American Indian students" means students identified by the state count of American Indian students on October 1 of the previous school year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.74.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/312/#laws.0.4.0">1977 c 312 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.138.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 138</a>-142,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.34.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 34</a>-38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.2.68.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 2 s 68</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.47.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 47</a>,48; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.4.9.0">2023 c 55 art 4 s 9</a>-12</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.75">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.75 LICENSES FOR AMERICAN INDIAN LANGUAGE AND CULTURE EDUCATION TEACHERS; EXEMPTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.75.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">American Indian language and culture education licenses.</span></h2>
          <p>The Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board, in consultation with the Tribal Nations Education Committee, must grant initial and continuing teaching licenses in American Indian language and culture education that bear the same duration as other initial and continuing licenses. The board must grant licenses to persons who present satisfactory evidence that they:</p>
          <p>(1) possess competence in an American Indian language or possess unique qualifications relative to or knowledge and understanding of American Indian history and culture; or</p>
          <p>(2) possess a bachelor's degree or other academic degree approved by the board or meet such requirements as to course of study and training as the board may prescribe, or possess such relevant experience as the board may prescribe.</p>
          <p>This evidence may be presented by affidavits, tribal resolutions, or by such other methods as the board may prescribe. Individuals may present applications for licensure on their own behalf or these applications may be submitted by the superintendent or other authorized official of a school district, participating school, or an American Indian school.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.75.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Persons holding general teaching licenses.</span></h2>
          <p>A person holding a general teaching license who presents the board with satisfactory evidence of competence in an American Indian language, or knowledge and understanding of American Indian history and culture may be licensed under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.75.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Resolution or letter.</span></h2>
          <p>All persons applying for a license under this section must submit to the board a resolution or letter of support signed by an American Indian tribal government. All persons holding a license under this section must have on file or file with the board a resolution or letter of support signed by a tribal government.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.75.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Employment of teachers.</span></h2>
          <p>Teachers employed in an American Indian language and culture education program pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a> shall not be employed to replace any presently employed teacher who otherwise would not be replaced.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.75.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Teacher preparation programs.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purpose of licensing American Indian language and culture education teachers, the board may approve programs at colleges or universities designed for their training subject to the approval of the commissioner of education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.75.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Persons eligible for employment; exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person licensed under this section shall be eligible for employment by a school board or a participating school as a teacher in an American Indian education program in which the American Indian language or culture in which the person is licensed is taught. A school district or participating school may prescribe only those additional qualifications for teachers licensed under this section as are approved by the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board. Any school board or participating school upon request may be exempted from the licensure requirements of this section in the hiring of one or more American Indian language and culture education teachers for any school year in which compliance would, in the opinion of the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board, create a hardship in the securing of the teachers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.75.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Persons serving under exemptions; licensure; tenure.</span></h2>
          <p>An American Indian language and culture education teacher serving under an exemption as provided in subdivision 6 shall be granted a license as soon as that teacher achieves the qualifications for the license. Not more than one year of service by an American Indian language and culture education teacher under an exemption shall be credited to the teacher for the purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a> and not more than two years shall be credited for the purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41">122A.41</a>; and the one or two years shall be deemed to precede immediately and be consecutive with the year in which a teacher becomes licensed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.75.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>A teacher holding a license or exemption under this section shall be compensated according to a schedule which is at least equivalent to that applicable to teachers holding general licenses.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.75.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Affirmative efforts in hiring.</span></h2>
          <p>In hiring for all positions in these programs, school districts and participating schools shall give preference to and make affirmative efforts to seek, recruit, and employ persons who share the culture of the American Indian children who are enrolled in the program. The district or participating school must provide procedures for the involvement of the parent advisory committees in the recruitment, screening, and selection of applicants. This subdivision shall not be construed to limit the school board's authority to hire and discharge personnel.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.75.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/312/#laws.0.5.0">1977 c 312 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.3.8.0">1988 c 718 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.3.9.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 3 s 9</a>; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.143.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 143</a>-146,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.39.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.49.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 49</a>-51; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.3.27.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 3 s 27</a>,28</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.76">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.76 AMERICAN INDIAN EDUCATION PROGRAM COORDINATORS, PARAPROFESSIONALS.</h1>
        <p>In addition to employing American Indian language and culture education teachers, each district or participating school providing programs pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82 </a>may employ paraprofessionals. Paraprofessionals must not be employed for the purpose of supplanting American Indian language and culture education teachers.</p>
        <p>Any district or participating school that conducts American Indian education programs pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a> must employ one or more full-time or part-time dedicated American Indian education program coordinators in a district with 100 or more state-identified American Indian students enrolled in the district. A dedicated American Indian education program coordinator must promote communication, understanding, and cooperation between the schools and the community and must visit the homes of children who are to be enrolled in an American Indian education program in order to convey information about the program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.76.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/312/#laws.0.6.0">1977 c 312 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.147.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 147</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.40.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.52.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.4.13.0">2023 c 55 art 4 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.77">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.77 RECRUITING AND RETAINING INDIAN TEACHERS.</h1>
        <p>This section applies to a board of a district in which there are at least ten American Indian children enrolled. The board must actively recruit teacher applicants who are American Indian from the time it is reasonably expected that a position will become available until the position is filled or September 1, whichever is earlier. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.7">122A.40, subdivision 7</a> or 10, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41">122A.41</a>, subdivision 4, any other law to the contrary, or any provision of a contract entered into after May 7, 1988, to the contrary, when placing a teacher on unrequested leave of absence, the board may retain a probationary teacher or a teacher with less seniority in order to retain an American Indian teacher.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.77.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.3.9.0">1988 c 718 art 3 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.148.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 148</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.30.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 30</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.78">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.78 PARENT AND COMMUNITY PARTICIPATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.78.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Parent committee.</span></h2>
          <p>School districts, charter schools, Tribal contract schools, and the respective school boards must provide for the maximum involvement of parents of American Indian children enrolled in American Indian education programs, programs for elementary and secondary grades, special education programs, and support services. Accordingly, school districts, charter schools, and Tribal contract schools in which there are ten or more state-identified American Indian students enrolled must establish an American Indian Parent Advisory Committee. If a committee whose membership consists of a majority of parents of American Indian children has been or is established according to federal, Tribal, or other state law, that committee may serve as the committee required by this section and is subject to, at least, the requirements of this subdivision and subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>The American Indian Parent Advisory Committee must develop its recommendations in consultation with the curriculum advisory committee required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11#stat.120B.11.3">120B.11, subdivision 3</a>. This committee must afford parents the necessary information and the opportunity effectively to express their views concerning all aspects of American Indian education and the educational needs of the American Indian children enrolled in the school or program. School districts, charter schools, and Tribal contract schools must ensure that programs are planned, operated, and evaluated with the involvement of and in consultation with parents of the American Indian students served by the programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.78.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Annual compliance.</span></h2>
          <p>Prior to March 1, the American Indian Parent Advisory Committee must meet to discuss whether or not they concur with the educational offerings that have been extended by the district to American Indian students. If the committee finds that the district, charter school, Tribal contract school, and the school board have been meeting the needs of American Indian students, they issue a vote and resolution of concurrence. If they find that the needs of American Indian students are not being met, they issue a vote and resolution of nonconcurrence. The vote and resolution must be presented to the school board by one or more members of the American Indian Parent Advisory Committee. The vote is formally reflected on documentation provided by the Department of Education and must be submitted annually on March 1.</p>
          <p>If the vote is one of nonconcurrence, the committee must provide written recommendations for improvement to the school board at the time of the presentation. In the case of nonconcurrence, the school board is given 60 days in which to respond, in writing, to the committee's recommendations. The board response must be signed by the entire school board and submitted to both the American Indian Parent Advisory Committee and to the Department of Education. The resolution must be accompanied by Parent Advisory Committee meeting minutes that show they have been appraised by the district on the goals of the Indian Education Program Plan and the measurement of progress toward those goals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.78.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>The American Indian Parent Advisory Committee must be composed of parents or guardians of American Indian children eligible to be enrolled in American Indian education programs; American Indian secondary students; American Indian family members of students eligible to be enrolled in American Indian education programs; American Indian language and culture education teachers and paraprofessionals; American Indian teachers; American Indian district employees; American Indian counselors; adult American Indian people enrolled in educational programs; and American Indian community members. The majority of each committee must be the parents or guardians of the American Indian children enrolled or eligible to be enrolled in the programs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.78.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Alternate committee.</span></h2>
          <p>If the organizational membership or the board of directors of a Tribal contract school consists of parents of children attending the school, that membership or board may serve also as the American Indian Parent Advisory Committee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.78.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">State-identified American Indian.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a>, the number of students who identify as American Indian or Alaska Native, as defined by the state of Minnesota on October 1 of the previous school year, will be used to determine the state-identified American Indian student counts for school districts, charter schools, and Tribal contract schools for the subsequent school year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.78.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/312/#laws.0.7.0">1977 c 312 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.3.10.0">1988 c 718 art 3 s 10</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.18.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.3.19.0">1994 c 647 art 3 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.1.71.0">1997 c 7 art 1 s 71</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.149.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 149</a>-151,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.41.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.53.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.2.19.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.4.14.0">2023 c 55 art 4 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.79">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.79 COMMUNITY AND COMMISSIONER PARTICIPATION IN AMERICAN INDIAN EDUCATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.79.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">American Indian community involvement.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must provide for the maximum involvement of the Tribal Nations Education Committee, parents of American Indian children, secondary students eligible to be served, American Indian language and culture education teachers, American Indian teachers, paraprofessionals, representatives of community groups, and persons knowledgeable in the field of American Indian education, in the formulation of policy and procedures relating to the administration of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a>. The commissioner must annually hold a field hearing on Indian education to gather input from American Indian educators, parents, and students on the state of American Indian education in Minnesota. Results of the hearing must be made available to all 11 tribal nations for review and comment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.79.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Technical assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide technical assistance to districts, schools and postsecondary institutions for preservice and in-service training for teachers, American Indian education teachers and paraprofessionals specifically designed to implement culturally responsive teaching methods, culturally based curriculum development, testing and testing mechanisms, the development of materials for American Indian education programs, and the annual report of American Indian student data using the state count.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.79.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application for funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall apply for money which may be available under federal programs for American Indian education, including funds for administration, demonstration projects, training, technical assistance, planning and evaluation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.79.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Consultation with the Tribal Nations Education Committee.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall seek consultation with the Tribal Nations Education Committee on all issues relating to American Indian education including:</p>
          <p>(1) administration of the commissioner's duties under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82 </a>and other programs;</p>
          <p>(2) administration of other programs for the education of American Indian people, as determined by the commissioner;</p>
          <p>(3) awarding of scholarships to eligible American Indian students;</p>
          <p>(4) administration of the commissioner's duties regarding awarding of American Indian postsecondary preparation grants to school districts; and</p>
          <p>(5) recommendations of education policy changes for American Indians.</p>
          <p>(b) Membership in the Tribal Nations Education Committee is the sole discretion of the committee and nothing in this subdivision gives the commissioner authority to dictate committee membership.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.79.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/312/#laws.0.8.0">1977 c 312 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/334/#laws.3.14.0">1979 c 334 art 3 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.3.8.0">1980 c 609 art 3 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.3.14.0">1988 c 718 art 3 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.13.48.0">1993 c 224 art 13 s 48</a>,49; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.152.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 152</a>,153,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.3.26.0">2013 c 116 art 3 s 26</a>,27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.54.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 54</a>,55; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.4.15.0">2023 c 55 art 4 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.791">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.791 INDIAN EDUCATION DIRECTOR.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.791.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Appointment.</span></h2>
          <p>An Indian education director shall be appointed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.791.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Qualifications.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall select the Indian education director on the basis of outstanding professional qualifications and knowledge of American Indian education, culture, practices, and beliefs. The Indian education director serves in the unclassified service. The commissioner may remove the Indian education director for cause. The commissioner is encouraged to seek qualified applicants who are enrolled members of a tribe.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.791.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Compensation.</span></h2>
          <p>Compensation of the Indian education director shall be established under chapter 15A.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.791.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Duties; powers.</span></h2>
          <p>The American Indian education director shall:</p>
          <p>(1) work collaboratively and in conjunction with the Tribal Liaison, the Tribal Nations Education Committee, the 11 Tribal nations in Minnesota, the Minnesota Chippewa Tribe, and the Minnesota Indian Affairs Council;</p>
          <p>(2) evaluate the state of American Indian education in Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(3) engage the Tribal bodies, community groups, parents of children eligible to be served by American Indian education programs, American Indian administrators and teachers, persons experienced in the training of teachers for American Indian education programs, the Tribally controlled schools, and other persons knowledgeable in the field of American Indian education and seek their advice on policies that can improve the quality of American Indian education;</p>
          <p>(4) advise the commissioner on American Indian education issues, including:</p>
          <p>(i) issues facing American Indian students;</p>
          <p>(ii) policies for American Indian education;</p>
          <p>(iii) awarding scholarships to eligible American Indian students and in administering the commissioner's duties regarding awarding of American Indian education grants to school districts; and</p>
          <p>(iv) administration of the commissioner's duties under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82 </a>and other programs for the education of American Indian people;</p>
          <p>(5) propose to the commissioner legislative changes that will improve the quality of American Indian education;</p>
          <p>(6) develop a strategic plan and a long-term framework for American Indian education, in conjunction with the Minnesota Indian Affairs Council, that is updated every five years and implemented by the commissioner, with goals to:</p>
          <p>(i) increase American Indian student achievement, including increased levels of proficiency and growth on statewide accountability assessments;</p>
          <p>(ii) increase the number of American Indian teachers in public schools;</p>
          <p>(iii) close the achievement gap between American Indian students and their more advantaged peers;</p>
          <p>(iv) increase the statewide graduation rate for American Indian students; and</p>
          <p>(v) increase American Indian student placement in postsecondary programs and the workforce; and</p>
          <p>(7) keep the American Indian community informed about the work of the department by reporting to the Tribal Nations Education Committee at each committee meeting.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.791.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.3.28.0">2013 c 116 art 3 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.56.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.4.16.0">2023 c 55 art 4 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.792">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.792 GRADUATION CEREMONIES; TRIBAL REGALIA AND OBJECTS OF CULTURAL SIGNIFICANCE.</h1>
        <p>A school district or charter school must not prohibit an American Indian student from wearing American Indian regalia, Tribal regalia, or objects of cultural significance at a graduation ceremony.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.792.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.4.17.0">2023 c 55 art 4 s 17</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.80"><b>124D.80</b> MS 2002 [Expired]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.81">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.81 AMERICAN INDIAN EDUCATION AID.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.81.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote"> Procedures.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district, charter school, cooperative unit as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, or American Indian-controlled Tribal contract or grant school enrolling at least 20 American Indian students identified by the state count on October 1 of the previous school year is eligible for American Indian education aid if it meets the requirements of this section. Programs may provide for contracts for the provision of program components by nonsectarian nonpublic, community, Tribal, charter, or alternative schools. The commissioner shall prescribe the form and manner of application for aids, and no aid shall be made for a program not complying with the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.81.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Plans.</span></h2>
          <p>To receive aid, an eligible district, charter school, cooperative unit as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, or Tribal contract school must develop and submit a plan for approval by the Indian education director that shall:</p>
          <p>(a) identify the measures to be used to meet the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a>;</p>
          <p>(b) identify the activities, methods and programs to meet the identified educational needs of the children to be enrolled in the program;</p>
          <p>(c) describe how district goals and objectives as well as the objectives of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a> are to be achieved;</p>
          <p>(d) demonstrate that required and elective courses as structured do not have a discriminatory effect within the meaning of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.74#stat.124D.74.5">124D.74, subdivision 5</a>;</p>
          <p>(e) describe how each school program will be organized, staffed, coordinated, and monitored; and</p>
          <p>(f) project expenditures for programs under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.81.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">American Indian education aid.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The American Indian education aid for an eligible district, cooperative unit, or Tribal contract school equals the greater of: (1) the sum of $40,000 plus the product of $500 times the difference between the number of American Indian students enrolled on October 1 of the previous school year and 20; or (2) if the district or school received a grant under this section for fiscal year 2015, the amount of the grant for fiscal year 2015.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding paragraph (a), the American Indian education aid must not exceed the district, cooperative unit, or Tribal contract school's actual expenditure according to the approved plan under subdivision 2, except as provided in subdivision 2b.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.81.2b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2b.<span class="headnote">Carry forward of funds.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.28">16A.28</a>, if a school district or Tribal contract school does not expend the full amount of the American Indian education aid in accordance with the plan in the designated fiscal year, the school district or Tribal contract school may carry forward and expend up to half of the remaining funds in the following fiscal year, and is not subject to an aid reduction if:</p>
          <p>(1) the district is otherwise following the plan submitted and approved under subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(2) the American Indian Parent Advisory Committee for the school is aware of and has approved the carry forward and has concurred with the district's educational offerings extended to American Indian students under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.78">124D.78</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) the funds carried over are used in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.74#stat.124D.74.1">124D.74, subdivision 1</a>; and</p>
          <p>(4) by April 1, the district reports to the Department of Education American Indian education director the reason the aid was not expended in the designated fiscal year, and describes how the district intends to expend the funds in the following fiscal year. The district must report this information in the form and manner determined by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.81.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Additional requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Each district or cooperative unit receiving aid under this section must each year conduct a count of American Indian children in the schools of the district; test for achievement; identify the extent of other educational needs of the children to be enrolled in the American Indian education program; and classify the American Indian children by grade, level of educational attainment, age and achievement. Participating schools must maintain records concerning the needs and achievements of American Indian children served.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.81.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Nondiscrimination; testing.</span></h2>
          <p>In accordance with recognized professional standards, all testing and evaluation materials and procedures utilized for the identification, testing, assessment, and classification of American Indian children must be selected and administered so as not to be racially or culturally discriminatory and must be valid for the purpose of identifying, testing, assessing, and classifying American Indian children.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.81.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Records.</span></h2>
          <p>Participating schools, districts, and cooperative units must keep records and afford access to them as the commissioner finds necessary to ensure that American Indian education programs are implemented in conformity with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a>. Each school district, cooperative unit, or participating school must keep accurate, detailed, and separate revenue and expenditure accounts for American Indian education programs funded under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.81.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Money from other sources.</span></h2>
          <p>A district, cooperative unit, or participating school providing American Indian education programs shall be eligible to receive moneys for these programs from other government agencies and from private sources when the moneys are available.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.81.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a> shall be construed as prohibiting a district, cooperative unit, or school from implementing an American Indian education program which is not in compliance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a> if the proposal and plan for that program is not funded pursuant to this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.81.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/312/#laws.0.10.0">1977 c 312 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/334/#laws.3.16.0">1979 c 334 art 3 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.3.10.0">1980 c 609 art 3 s 10</a>-12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.3.19.0">1981 c 358 art 3 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/314/#laws.3.12.0">1983 c 314 art 3 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.3.26.0">1987 c 398 art 3 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.13.50.0">1993 c 224 art 13 s 50</a>; art 14 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.156.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 156</a>-161,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.32.0">2000 c 254 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.42.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 42</a>-46; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.2.69.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 2 s 69</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.57.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.4.18.0">2023 c 55 art 4 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.4.2.0">2024 c 115 art 4 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.82">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.82 DISCRIMINATION PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <p>Nothing in the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a> shall be construed to violate the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.30">123B.30</a> or chapter 363A. Programs and activities pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.71">124D.71</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.82">124D.82</a> shall be deemed to be positive action programs to combat discrimination.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.82.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/312/#laws.0.11.0">1977 c 312 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.164.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 164</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.83">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.83 STATE REVENUE FOR AMERICAN INDIAN TRIBAL CONTRACT OR GRANT SCHOOLS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.83.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each year each American Indian-controlled tribal contract or grant school authorized by the United States Code, title 25, section 450f, that is located on a reservation within the state is eligible to receive tribal contract or grant school aid subject to the requirements in paragraphs (b) to (d).</p>
          <p>(b) The school must plan, conduct, and administer an education program that complies with the requirements of either this chapter and chapters 120A, 120B, 121A, 122A, 123A, 123B, 125A, 125B, 126C, 127A, 129, and 268A or Code of Federal Regulations, title 25, sections 31.0 to 45.80.</p>
          <p>(c) The school must comply with all other state statutes governing independent school districts or their equivalent in the Code of Federal Regulations, title 25.</p>
          <p>(d) The state tribal contract or grant school aid must be used to supplement, and not to replace, the money for American Indian education programs provided by the federal government.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.83.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Revenue amount.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">An American Indian-controlled tribal contract or grant school that is located on a reservation within the state and that complies with the requirements in subdivision 1 is eligible to receive tribal contract or grant school aid. The amount of aid is derived by:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) multiplying the formula allowance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, less $170, times the difference between (i) the resident pupil units as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.6">126C.05, subdivision 6</a>, in average daily membership, excluding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.13">126C.05, subdivision 13</a>, and (ii) the number of pupils for the current school year, weighted according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.1">126C.05, subdivision 1</a>, receiving benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.42">123B.42</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.44">123B.44</a> or for which the school is receiving reimbursement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.69">124D.69</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) adding to the result in clause (1) an amount equal to the product of the formula allowance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, less $300 times the tribal contract compensation revenue pupil units;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) subtracting from the result in clause (2) the amount of money allotted to the school by the federal government through Indian School Equalization Program of the Bureau of Indian Affairs, according to Code of Federal Regulations, title 25, part 39, subparts A to E, for the basic program as defined by section 39.11, paragraph (b), for the base rate as applied to kindergarten through twelfth grade, excluding small school adjustments and additional weighting, but not money allotted through subparts F to L for contingency funds, school board training, student training, interim maintenance and minor repair, interim administration cost, prekindergarten, and operation and maintenance, and the amount of money that is received according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.69">124D.69</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) dividing the result in clause (3) by the sum of the resident pupil units in average daily membership, excluding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.13">126C.05, subdivision 13</a>, plus the tribal contract compensation revenue pupil units; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) multiplying the sum of the resident pupil units, including section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.13">126C.05, subdivision 13</a>, in average daily membership plus the tribal contract compensation revenue pupil units by the lesser of $3,230 for fiscal year 2019 and 51.17 percent of the formula allowance for fiscal year 2020 and later or the result in clause (4).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.83.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Waiver.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 1, paragraphs (b) and (c), a tribal contract or grant school:</p>
          <p>(1) is not subject to the Minnesota Election Law;</p>
          <p>(2) has no authority under this section to levy for property taxes, issue and sell bonds, or incur debt; and</p>
          <p>(3) may request through its managing tribal organization a recommendation of the commissioner of education, for consideration of the legislature, that a tribal contract or grant school not be subject to specified statutes related to independent school districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.83.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Early childhood family education revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>A school receiving aid under this section may apply annually to the commissioner to receive an early childhood family education grant to provide early childhood family education programs for parents and children who are enrolled or eligible for enrollment in a federally recognized tribe. The grant must be used for programs and services that comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.10">142D.10</a>, except that the school is not required to provide a community education program or establish a community education advisory council. The program must be designed to improve the skills of parents and promote American Indian history, language, and culture. The school must make affirmative efforts to encourage participation by fathers. Admission may not be limited to those enrolled in or eligible for enrollment in a federally recognized tribe.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.83.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Tribal contract pupil units.</span></h2>
          <p>Pupil units for pupils enrolled in tribal contract schools shall be used only for the purpose of computing tribal contract aid according to this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.83.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.3.15.0">1989 c 329 art 3 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/426/#laws.1.16.0">1990 c 426 art 1 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.3.4.0">1990 c 562 art 3 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.16.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.9.11.0">1994 c 647 art 9 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.3.30.0">1996 c 412 art 3 s 30</a>,31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.29.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 29</a>,30; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.117.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 117</a>-120,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.33.0">2000 c 254 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.2.51.0">2009 c 96 art 2 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.58.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.2.38.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 2 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.2.20.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.26.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.42.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.124D.84">
        <h1>124D.84 </h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.84.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered 136A.126]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.84.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.2.56.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 2 s 56</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.85"><b>124D.85</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.78.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 78</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SCHOOL DESEGREGATION AND INTEGRATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.855">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.855 SCHOOL SEGREGATION PROHIBITED.</h1>
        <p>The state, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.30">123B.30</a> and chapter 363A, does not condone separating school children of different socioeconomic, demographic, ethnic, or racial backgrounds into distinct public schools. Instead, the state's interest lies in offering children a diverse and nondiscriminatory educational experience.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.855.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.42.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 42</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.86"><b>124D.86</b> MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.51.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 51</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.861">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.861 ACHIEVEMENT AND INTEGRATION FOR MINNESOTA.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.861.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program to close the academic achievement and opportunity gap; revenue uses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The "Achievement and Integration for Minnesota" program is established to pursue racial and economic integration and increase student academic achievement, create equitable educational opportunities, and reduce academic disparities based on students' diverse racial, ethnic, and economic backgrounds in Minnesota public schools.</p>
          <p>(b) For purposes of this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.862">124D.862</a>, "eligible district" means a district required to submit a plan to the commissioner under Minnesota Rules governing school desegregation and integration, or be a member of a multidistrict integration collaborative that files a plan with the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) Eligible districts must use the revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.862">124D.862</a> to pursue academic achievement and racial and economic integration through:</p>
          <p>(1) integrated learning environments that give students improved and equitable access to effective and more diverse teachers, prepare all students to be effective citizens, and enhance social cohesion;</p>
          <p>(2) policies and curricula and trained instructors, administrators, school counselors, and other advocates to support and enhance integrated learning environments under this section, including through magnet schools, innovative, research-based instruction, differentiated instruction, improved and equitable access to effective and diverse teachers, and targeted interventions to improve achievement; and</p>
          <p>(3) rigorous career and college readiness programs and effective and more diverse instructors for underserved student populations, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.307">120B.307</a>; integrated learning environments to increase student academic achievement; cultural fluency, competency, and interaction; graduation and educational attainment rates; and parent involvement.</p>
          <p>(d) Consistent with paragraph (c), eligible districts may adopt policies to increase the diversity of district teachers and administrators using the revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.862">124D.862 </a>for recruitment, retention, and hiring incentives or additional compensation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.861.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Plan implementation; components.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The school board of each eligible district must formally develop and implement a long-term plan under this section. The plan must be incorporated into the district's comprehensive strategic plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The plan must contain goals for:</p>
          <p>(1) reducing the disparities in academic achievement and in equitable access to effective and more diverse teachers among all students and specific categories of students under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.35#stat.120B.35.3">120B.35, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (b), excluding the student categories of gender, disability, and English learners; and</p>
          <p>(2) increasing racial and economic diversity and integration in schools and districts.</p>
          <p>(c) The plan must include strategies to validate, affirm, embrace, and integrate cultural and community strengths of all students, families, and employees in the district's curriculum as well as learning and work environments. The plan must address issues of institutional racism as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11#stat.120B.11.1">120B.11, subdivision 1</a>, in schools that create opportunity and achievement gaps for students, families, and staff who are of color or who are American Indian. Examples of institutional racism experienced by students who are of color or who are American Indian include policies and practices that intentionally or unintentionally result in disparate discipline referrals and suspension, inequitable access to advanced coursework, overrepresentation in lower-level coursework, inequitable participation in cocurricular activities, inequitable parent involvement, and lack of equitable access to racially and ethnically diverse teachers who reflect the racial or ethnic diversity of students because it has not been a priority to hire or retain such teachers.</p>
          <p>(d) School districts must use local data, to the extent practicable, to develop plan components and strategies. Plans may include:</p>
          <p>(1) innovative and integrated prekindergarten through grade 12 learning environments that offer students school enrollment choices;</p>
          <p>(2) family engagement initiatives that involve families in their students' academic life and success and improve relations between home and school;</p>
          <p>(3) opportunities for students, families, staff, and community members who are of color or American Indian to share their experiences in the school setting with school staff and administration and to inform the development of specific proposals for making school environments more validating, affirming, embracing, and integrating of their cultural and community strengths;</p>
          <p>(4) professional development opportunities for teachers and administrators focused on improving the academic achievement of all students, including knowledge, skills, and dispositions needed to be antiracist and culturally sustaining as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a>, subdivision 1, for serving students who are from racially and ethnically diverse backgrounds;</p>
          <p>(5) recruitment and retention of teachers, administrators, cultural and family liaisons, paraprofessionals, and other staff from racial, ethnic, and linguistic backgrounds represented in the student population to strengthen relationships with all students, families, and other members of the community;</p>
          <p>(6) collection, examination, and evaluation of academic and discipline data for institutional racism as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11#stat.120B.11.1">120B.11, subdivision 1</a>, in structures, policies, and practices that result in the education disparities, in order to propose antiracist changes as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11#stat.120B.11.1">120B.11, subdivision 1</a>, that increase access, meaningful participation, representation, and positive outcomes for students of color and American Indian students;</p>
          <p>(7) increased programmatic opportunities and effective and more diverse instructors focused on rigor and college and career readiness for students who are impacted by racial, gender, linguistic, and economic disparities, including students enrolled in area learning centers or alternative learning programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.05">123A.05</a>, state-approved alternative programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.05#stat.126C.05.15">126C.05, subdivision 15</a>, and contract alternative programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.69">124D.69</a>, among other underserved students;</p>
          <p>(8) instruction in ethnic studies, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.25">120B.25</a>, to provide all students with opportunities to learn about their own and others' cultures and historical experiences; or</p>
          <p>(9) examination and revision of district curricula in all subjects to be inclusive of diverse racial and ethnic groups while meeting state academic standards and being culturally sustaining as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11#stat.120B.11.1">120B.11, subdivision 1</a>, ensuring content being studied about any group is accurate and based in knowledge from that group.</p>
          <p>(e) Among other requirements, an eligible district must implement effective, research-based interventions that include multiple measures of assessment and engagement in order to eliminate academic disparities for students impacted by racial, gender, linguistic, and economic inequities as aligned with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a>.</p>
          <p>(f) Eligible districts must create efficiencies and eliminate duplicative programs and services under this section, which may include forming collaborations or a single, seven-county metropolitan areawide partnership of eligible districts for this purpose.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.861.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Public engagement; progress report and budget process.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To receive revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.862">124D.862</a>, the school board of an eligible district must incorporate school and district plan components under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a> into the district's comprehensive integration plan.</p>
          <p>(b) A school board must hold at least one formal annual hearing to publicly report its progress in realizing the goals identified in its plan. At the hearing, the board must provide the public with longitudinal data demonstrating district and school progress in reducing the disparities in student academic performance among the specified categories of students, in improving students' equitable access to effective and more diverse teachers, and in realizing racial and economic diversity and integration, consistent with the district plan and the measures in paragraph (a). At least 30 days before the formal hearing under this paragraph, the board must post its plan, its preliminary analysis, relevant student performance data, and other longitudinal data on the district's website. A district must hold one hearing to meet the hearing requirements of both this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The district must submit a detailed budget to the commissioner by March 15 in the year before it implements its plan. The commissioner must review, and approve or disapprove the district's budget by June 1 of that year.</p>
          <p>(d) The longitudinal data required under paragraph (b) must be based on student growth and progress in reading and mathematics, as defined under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.303">120B.303</a>, and student performance data and achievement reports from fully adaptive reading and mathematics assessments for grades 3 through 7 beginning in the 2015-2016 school year under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.302">120B.302</a>, and either (i) school enrollment choices, (ii) the number of world language proficiency or high achievement certificates awarded under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.022">120B.022, subdivision</a> 1a, or the number of state bilingual and multilingual seals issued under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.022#stat.120B.022.1b">120B.022, subdivision 1b</a>, or (iii) school safety and students' engagement and connection at school under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.35#stat.120B.35.3">120B.35, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (d). Additional longitudinal data may be based on: students' progress toward career and college readiness under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.307">120B.307</a>; or rigorous coursework completed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.35#stat.120B.35.3">120B.35, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (c), clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.861.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Timeline and implementation.</span></h2>
          <p>A board must approve its plan and submit it to the department by March 15. If a district that is part of a multidistrict council applies for revenue for a plan, the individual district shall not receive revenue unless it ratifies the plan adopted by the multidistrict council. Each plan has a term of three years. For the 2014-2015 school year, an eligible district under this section must submit its plan to the commissioner for review by March 15, 2014. For the 2013-2014 school year only, an eligible district may continue to implement its current plan until the commissioner approves a new plan under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.861.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Evaluation.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must evaluate the efficacy of district plans in reducing the disparities in student academic performance among the specified categories of students within the district, improving students' equitable access to effective and diverse teachers, and in realizing racial and economic diversity and integration. The commissioner shall report evaluation results to the kindergarten through grade 12 education committees of the legislature by February 1 of every odd-numbered year.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.861.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.3.29.0">2013 c 116 art 3 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.1.38.0">2014 c 272 art 1 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.20.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.24.19.0">2016 c 189 art 24 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.2.56.0">2023 c 55 art 2 s 56</a>,65</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.862">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.862 ACHIEVEMENT AND INTEGRATION REVENUE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.862.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Initial achievement and integration revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An eligible district's initial achievement and integration revenue equals the lesser of 100.3 percent of the district's expenditures under the budget approved by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.861#stat.124D.861.3">124D.861, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (c), excluding expenditures used to generate incentive revenue under subdivision 2, or the sum of (1) $350 times the district's adjusted pupil units for that year times the ratio of the district's enrollment of protected students for the previous school year to total enrollment for the previous school year and (2) the greater of zero or 66 percent of the difference between the district's integration revenue for fiscal year 2013 and the district's integration revenue for fiscal year 2014 under clause (1).</p>
          <p>(b) In each year, an amount equal to 0.3 percent of each district's initial achievement and integration revenue for the second prior fiscal year is transferred to the department for the oversight and accountability activities required under this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.861">124D.861</a>.</p>
        </div>
