        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.862.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Incentive revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>An eligible school district's maximum incentive revenue equals $10 per adjusted pupil unit. A district's incentive revenue equals the lesser of the maximum incentive revenue or the district's expenditures for implementing a voluntary plan to reduce racial and economic enrollment disparities through intradistrict and interdistrict activities that have been approved as a part of the district's achievement and integration plan under the budget approved by the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.861#stat.124D.861.3">124D.861, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.862.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Achievement and integration revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Achievement and integration revenue equals the sum of initial achievement and integration revenue and incentive revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.862.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Achievement and integration aid.</span></h2>
          <p>For fiscal year 2015 and later, a district's achievement and integration aid equals 70 percent of its achievement and integration revenue.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.862.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Achievement and integration levy.</span></h2>
          <p>A district's achievement and integration levy equals its achievement and integration revenue times 30 percent. For Special School District No. 1, Minneapolis; Independent School District No. 625, St. Paul; and Independent School District No. 709, Duluth, 100 percent of the levy certified under this subdivision is shifted into the prior calendar year for purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.75#stat.123B.75.5">123B.75, subdivision 5</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.441">127A.441</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.862.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Revenue uses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) At least 80 percent of a district's achievement and integration revenue received under this section must be used for innovative and integrated learning environments, school enrollment choices, family engagement activities, and other approved programs providing direct services to students.</p>
          <p>(b) Up to 20 percent of the revenue may be used for professional development and staff development activities and placement services.</p>
          <p>(c) No more than ten percent of the total amount of revenue may be spent on administrative services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.862.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Revenue reserved.</span></h2>
          <p>Integration revenue received under this section must be reserved and used only for the programs authorized in subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.862.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Commissioner authority to withhold revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must review the results of each district's integration and achievement plan by August 1 at the end of the third year of implementing the plan and determine if the district met its goals.</p>
          <p>(b) If a district met its goals, it may submit a new three-year plan to the commissioner for review.</p>
          <p>(c) If a district has not met its goals, the commissioner must:</p>
          <p>(1) guide the district in the development of an improvement plan and timeline that identifies strategies and practices designed to meet the district's goals under this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) use up to 20 percent of the district's integration revenue, until the district's goals are reached, to implement the improvement plan.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.862.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.3.30.0">2013 c 116 art 3 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/143/#laws.3.1.0">2013 c 143 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/144/#laws.0.16.0">2013 c 144 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.16.6.0">2014 c 312 art 16 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.2.21.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 2 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.2.57.0">2023 c 55 art 2 s 57</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.87">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.87 ACHIEVEMENT AND INTEGRATION TRANSPORTATION AID.</h1>
        <p>(a) A district that provides transportation of pupils to and from an interdistrict program for desegregation or integration purposes is eligible for state aid to reimburse the additional costs of transportation during the preceding fiscal year.</p>
        <p>(b) A district in the metropolitan area may apply to the commissioner for state aid to reimburse the costs of transporting pupils who are enrolled under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.03">124D.03</a> during the preceding fiscal year if the enrollment of the student in the nonresident district contributes to desegregation or integration purposes. The commissioner shall develop the form and manner of applications for state aid, the criteria to be used to determine when transportation is for desegregation or integration purposes, and the accounting procedure to be used to determine excess costs. In determining aid amounts, the commissioner shall consider other revenue received by the district for transportation for desegregation or integration purposes.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.87.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.2.50.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 2 s 50</a>; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.2.13.0">1996 c 412 art 2 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.2.88.0">1998 c 397 art 2 s 88</a>,164; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.2.12.0">1999 c 241 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.2.11.0">2000 c 489 art 2 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.871"><b>124D.871</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.51.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 51</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.88"><b>124D.88</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.51.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 51</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.89"><b>124D.89</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.2.56.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 2 s 56</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.892">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.892 OFFICE OF DESEGREGATION/INTEGRATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.892.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An Office of Desegregation/Integration is established in the Department of Education to coordinate and support activities related to student enrollment, student and staff recruitment and retention, transportation, and interdistrict cooperation among school districts.</p>
          <p>(b) At the request of a school district involved in cooperative desegregation/integration efforts, the office shall perform any of the following activities:</p>
          <p>(1) assist districts with interdistrict student transfers, including student recruitment, counseling, placement, and transportation;</p>
          <p>(2) coordinate and disseminate information about schools and programs;</p>
          <p>(3) assist districts with new magnet schools and programs;</p>
          <p>(4) assist districts in providing staff development and in-service training; and</p>
          <p>(5) coordinate and administer staff exchanges.</p>
          <p>(c) The office shall collect data on the efficacy of districts' desegregation/integration efforts and make recommendations based on the data. The office shall periodically consult with the Metropolitan Council to coordinate metropolitan school desegregation/integration efforts with the housing, social, economic, and infrastructure needs of the metropolitan area. The office shall develop a process for resolving students' disputes and grievances about student transfers under a desegregation/integration plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.892.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Coordination.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may request information or assistance from, or contract with, any state or local agency or officer, local unit of government, or recognized expert to assist the commissioner in performing the activities described in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.892.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 2002 [Expired, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.51.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.892.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.8.2.0">1994 c 647 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.3.3.0">1997 c 7 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.4.51.0">1998 c 397 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.6.13.0">1998 c 398 art 6 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.3.7.0">2000 c 489 art 3 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/7/#laws.0.32.0">2001 c 7 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/6/#laws.2.50.0">1Sp2001 c 6 art 2 s 50</a>,51; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.894"><b>124D.894</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.58.0">2001 c 161 s 58</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.895">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.895 PARENTAL INVOLVEMENT PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.895.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program goals.</span></h2>
          <p>The department, in consultation with the state curriculum advisory committee, must develop guidelines and model plans for parental involvement programs that will:</p>
          <p>(1) engage the interests and talents of parents or guardians in recognizing and meeting the emotional, intellectual, native and English language development, and physical needs of their school-age children;</p>
          <p>(2) promote healthy self-concepts among parents or guardians and other family members;</p>
          <p>(3) offer parents or guardians a chance to share and learn about educational skills, techniques, and ideas;</p>
          <p>(4) provide creative learning experiences for parents or guardians and their school-age children, including involvement from parents or guardians of color;</p>
          <p>(5) encourage parents to actively participate in their district's curriculum advisory committee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a> in order to assist the school board in improving children's education programs;</p>
          <p>(6) encourage parents to help in promoting school desegregation/integration under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.861">124D.861</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.862">124D.862</a>; and</p>
          <p>(7) partner with parents in establishing a positive school climate by developing and implementing prevention and intervention programs on prohibited conduct under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.031">121A.031</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.895.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Plan contents.</span></h2>
          <p>Model plans for a parental involvement program must include at least the following:</p>
          <p>(1) program goals;</p>
          <p>(2) means for achieving program goals;</p>
          <p>(3) methods for informing parents or guardians, in a timely way, about the program;</p>
          <p>(4) strategies for ensuring the full participation of parents or guardians, including those parents or guardians who lack literacy skills or whose native language is not English, including the involvement of parents or guardians of color;</p>
          <p>(5) procedures for coordinating the program with kindergarten through grade 12 curriculum, with parental involvement programs currently available in the community, with comprehensive achievement and civic readiness under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a>, and with other education facilities located in the community;</p>
          <p>(6) strategies for training teachers and other school staff to work effectively with parents and guardians;</p>
          <p>(7) procedures for parents or guardians and educators to evaluate and report progress toward program goals; and</p>
          <p>(8) a mechanism for convening a local community advisory committee composed primarily of parents or guardians to advise a district on implementing a parental involvement program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.895.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Plan activities.</span></h2>
          <p>Activities contained in the model plans must include:</p>
          <p>(1) educational opportunities for families that enhance children's learning and native and English language development;</p>
          <p>(2) educational programs for parents or guardians on families' educational responsibilities and resources;</p>
          <p>(3) the hiring, training, and use of parental involvement liaison workers to coordinate family involvement activities and to foster linguistic and culturally competent communication among families, educators, and students, consistent with the definition of culturally competent under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.30#stat.120B.30.8">120B.30, subdivision 8</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) curriculum materials and assistance in implementing home and community-based learning activities that reinforce and extend classroom instruction and student motivation;</p>
          <p>(5) technical assistance, including training to design and carry out family involvement programs;</p>
          <p>(6) parent resource centers;</p>
          <p>(7) parent training programs and reasonable and necessary expenditures associated with parents' attendance at training sessions;</p>
          <p>(8) reports to parents on children's progress;</p>
          <p>(9) use of parents as classroom volunteers, or as volunteers in before and after school programs for school-age children, tutors, and aides;</p>
          <p>(10) soliciting parents' suggestions in planning, developing, and implementing school programs;</p>
          <p>(11) educational programs and opportunities for parents or guardians that are multicultural, multilingual, gender fair, and disability sensitive;</p>
          <p>(12) involvement in a district's curriculum advisory committee or a site team under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.11">120B.11</a>; and</p>
          <p>(13) opportunities for parent involvement in developing, implementing, or evaluating school and district desegregation/integration plans under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.861">124D.861</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.862">124D.862</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.895.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.8.34.0">1990 c 562 art 8 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.37.0">1991 c 130 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.8.7.0">1992 c 499 art 8 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.4.32.0">1994 c 647 art 4 s 32</a>,33; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.6.119.0">1998 c 397 art 6 s 119</a>,124; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.34.0">2000 c 254 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/160/#laws.0.4.0">2014 c 160 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.1.39.0">2014 c 272 art 1 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/275/#laws.1.19.0">2014 c 275 art 1 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.44.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.2.65.0">2023 c 55 art 2 s 65</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/109/#laws.2.31.0">2024 c 109 art 2 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.8955">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.8955 PARENT AND FAMILY INVOLVEMENT POLICY.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) In order to promote and support student achievement, a local school board is encouraged to formally adopt and implement a parent and family involvement policy that promotes and supports:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) oral and written communication between home and school that is regular, two-way, meaningful, and in families' native language;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) parenting skills;</p>
        <p class="in">(3) parents and caregivers who play an integral role in assisting student learning and learn about fostering students' academic success and learning at home and school;</p>
        <p class="in">(4) welcoming parents in the school and using networks that support families' cultural connections, seeking their support and assistance;</p>
        <p class="in">(5) partnerships with parents in the decisions that affect children and families in the schools; and</p>
        <p class="in">(6) providing community resources to strengthen schools, families, and student learning, including establishing a safe and supportive school climate by developing and implementing prevention and intervention programs on prohibited conduct under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.031">121A.031</a>.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) A school board that implements a parent and family involvement policy under paragraph (a) must convene an advisory committee composed of an equal number of resident parents who are not district employees and school staff to make recommendations to the board on developing and evaluating the board's parent and family involvement policy. If possible, the advisory committee must represent the diversity of the district. The advisory committee must consider the district's demographic diversity and barriers to parent involvement when developing its recommendations. The advisory committee must recommend to the school board and district or school how programs serving children and adolescents can collaborate on:</p>
        <p>(1) understanding child and adolescent development;</p>
        <p>(2) encouraging healthy communication between parents and children;</p>
        <p>(3) managing students' behavior through positive reinforcement;</p>
        <p>(4) establishing expectations for student behavior;</p>
        <p>(5) providing media and Internet limits and supervision; and</p>
        <p>(6) promoting resilience and reducing risks for children.</p>
        <p class="bl">The advisory committee must present its recommendations to the board for board consideration.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) The board must consider research-based best practices when implementing this policy.</p>
        <p class="in">(d) The board periodically must review this policy to determine whether it is aligned with the most current research findings on parent involvement policies and practices and how effective the policy is in supporting increased student achievement.</p>
        <p class="in">(e) Nothing in this section obligates a school district to exceed any parent or family involvement requirement under federal law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.8955.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.2.29.0">2007 c 146 art 2 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/160/#laws.0.5.0">2014 c 160 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.1.40.0">2014 c 272 art 1 s 40</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.8957">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.8957 PREKINDERGARTEN THROUGH GRADE 12 PARENTAL RIGHTS CODED ELSEWHERE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>The sections referred to in subdivisions 2 to 30 are codified outside this section. Those sections include many but not all the sections governing parental rights related to topics in prekindergarten through grade 12 education.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Compulsory instruction.</span></h2>
          <p>Parental rights related to compulsory instruction, including the right to withdraw a child from school; to receive notice related to transfer of disciplinary records; to excuse a child from school for illnesses, appointments, or religious events; and the right of noncustodial parents to access school records and conferences, among other rights, are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22">120A.22</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Longitudinal data.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to annual summary longitudinal performance and progress data is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.31">120B.31</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Antibullying.</span></h2>
          <p>Parental rights related to school district antibullying policies, including the right to be involved in developing the policies, the right to be notified of incidents of prohibited conduct, and the right to be informed of data practices laws, are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.031">121A.031</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Student discipline policies.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to notice in student discipline policies of rights under the Safe and Supportive Minnesota Schools Act is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.0311">121A.0311</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Early childhood development screening.</span></h2>
          <p>Parental rights to certain notice requirements related to early childhood development screening and to receive results of early childhood development screening are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.091">142D.091</a>. The parental right to provide consent before individual screening data may be disclosed to a school district is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.092">142D.092</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Chemical abuse.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to be informed of a reported case of chemical abuse by a minor student is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.26">121A.26</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Pesticides.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to be notified regarding the use of pesticides at a school is governed by the Janet B. Johnson Parents' Right-to-Know Act under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.30">121A.30</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Student dismissal.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to notice and a meeting regarding the removal of a student for more than ten days is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.45">121A.45</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Exclusion and expulsion.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to be included in exclusion or expulsion hearing procedures, including access to records, ability to testify and present evidence, and inclusion in the student's readmission plan, is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.47">121A.47</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Exclusion and expulsion appeal.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to notice of the right to appeal an exclusion or expulsion decision is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.49">121A.49</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Reinstatement after termination of dismissal.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to notice of a student's right to be reinstated after the termination of dismissal is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.54">121A.54</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Interdistrict cooperation.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to notice of an informational school board meeting relating to discontinuing interdistrict cooperation is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.32">123A.32</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Background checks.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to notice of a school's background check policy for hiring teachers is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.03">123B.03</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Textbook fees.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to notice of a school board's policy to charge fees for textbooks lost or destroyed by students is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.37">123B.37</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.<span class="headnote">Transportation privileges.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to surrender a student's privilege to receive transportation services from a school district is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.88">123B.88</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.17">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 17.<span class="headnote">Nonresident district policies.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to receive notice of: a decision on an application by a student to attend school in a nonresident district; the transportation policies of the nonresident district; and the right to be reimbursed for costs of transportation to the nonresident district's border is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.03">124D.03</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.18">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 18.<span class="headnote">Out-of-state districts.</span></h2>
          <p>Under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.04">124D.04</a>, the parental rights related to a student attending a nonresident district under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.03">124D.03</a> apply to a student attending an out-of-state district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.19">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 19.<span class="headnote">Free or reduced-price meals eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to opt a child out of disclosing a child's eligibility for free or reduced-price meals to the Department of Education and the Department of Human Services is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.1115">124D.1115</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.20">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 20.<span class="headnote">Learning year programs.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to notice of optional learning year programs is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.128">124D.128</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.21">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 21.<span class="headnote">English learners programs.</span></h2>
          <p>Parental rights related to student enrollment in programs for English learners, including notice, withdrawal, and parental involvement, are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.60">124D.60</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.<span class="headnote">Charter school transportation.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to receive pupil transportation information from the charter school or school district providing transportation services to a charter school student is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.88">123B.88</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.23">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 23.<span class="headnote">Services for children with disabilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to be included in determining the appropriate and necessary services for students with disabilities is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.027">125A.027</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.24">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24.</h2>
          <p>MS 2016 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.1.109.0">2018 c 182 art 1 s 109</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.25">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25.<span class="headnote">Special education alternative dispute resolution.</span></h2>
          <p>Parental rights regarding notice, participation, and due process related to special education alternative dispute resolution procedures are governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.091">125A.091</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.26">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26.<span class="headnote">Third-party reimbursement for children with disabilities.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to notice of a school district seeking reimbursement from medical assistance or MinnesotaCare for services rendered to a student with a disability is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.21">125A.21</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.27">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27.<span class="headnote">Services provided to children with disabilities.</span></h2>
          <p>Parental rights related to services provided to students eligible for Part C services under the Individuals with Disabilities Education Act and the right to receive written materials regarding the implementation of Part C services are governed by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.42">125A.42</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.48">125A.48</a>. The parental right to use mediation to resolve disputes under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.42">125A.42</a> is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.43">125A.43</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.28">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 28.<span class="headnote">Minnesota State Academies discharge.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to notice of a student's discharge from the Minnesota State Academies is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.68">125A.68</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.29">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 29.<span class="headnote">Education records for military children.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to education records under the Interstate Compact on Educational Opportunity for Military Children is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.85">127A.85</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.8957.30">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 30.<span class="headnote">Appeal adverse school board decision.</span></h2>
          <p>The parental right to appeal a school board decision adversely affecting an academic program of an enrolled student is governed by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/129C.10#stat.129C.10.3b">129C.10, subdivision 3b</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.8957.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/189/#laws.25.37.0">2016 c 189 art 25 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.9.19.0">2023 c 55 art 9 s 19;</a> <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.4.26.0">2024 c 80 art 4 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.42.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 42</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.896">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.896 DESEGREGATION/INTEGRATION AND INCLUSIVE EDUCATION RULES.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner shall propose rules relating to desegregation/integration and inclusive education, consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.861">124D.861 </a>and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.862">124D.862</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) In adopting a rule related to school desegregation/integration, the commissioner shall address the need for equal educational opportunities for all students and racial balance as defined by the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.896.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.2.11.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/718/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 718 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1975/0/162/#laws.0.6.0">1975 c 162 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/271/#laws.0.21.0">1976 c 271 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/347/#laws.0.19.0">1977 c 347 s 19</a>;       <h1 class="fgh">ADDITIONAL PROGRAMS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.90">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.90 SCHOOL ENRICHMENT PARTNERSHIP PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.90.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The school enrichment partnership program is established. The purpose of the program is to encourage districts to expand the involvement of the private sector in the delivery of academic programs. The program will provide matching state funds for those provided by the private sector.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.90.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Revenue eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>A district or group of districts is eligible to receive state aid under this program. Districts may enter into joint agreements to provide programs or make expenditures under this section. The limitations under this subdivision apply to these programs or expenditures as if they were operated by a single district. A district may receive $1 of state aid for each $2 raised from the private sector. The private match must be in the form of cash. Specific types of noncash support may be considered for the private match. State aid is limited to the lesser of $75,000 or $10 per pupil unit per district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.90.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Revenue management.</span></h2>
          <p>The use of the state and private funds provided under this section is under the general control of the board. The board may establish, without using state funds or public employees, a separate foundation to directly manage the funds. The private funds must be used to acquire instructional or noninstructional academic materials of a capital nature including, but not limited to, textbooks, globes, maps, and other academic material. The funds shall not be used for salaries or other employee benefits.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.90.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Procedures; report.</span></h2>
          <p> The commissioner must establish application forms, guidelines, procedures, and timelines for the distribution of state aid. The commissioner may require reporting necessary to evaluate the program. Measures of success will include numbers of partnerships and funds raised; numbers of school foundations formed; and demonstrated linkages of partnerships to improved instructional delivery resulting in increased student learning.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.90.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Results-oriented charter schools.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124E.24">124E.24</a>, paragraph (b), a results-oriented charter school is eligible to participate in the program under this section as if it were a district.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.90.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.16.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 16</a>; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.3.68.0">1998 c 397 art 3 s 68</a>,103; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.4.10.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 4 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/187/#laws.0.1.0">2016 c 187 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.901">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.901 STUDENT SUPPORT PERSONNEL AID.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.901.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given:</p>
          <p>(1) "new position" means a student support services personnel full-time or part-time position not under contract by a school district, charter school, or cooperative unit at the start of the 2022-2023 school year;</p>
          <p>(2) "part-time position" means a student support services personnel position less than 1.0 full-time equivalent at the start of the 2022-2023 school year;</p>
          <p>(3) "American Rescue Plan Act" means the federal American Rescue Plan Act of 2021, Public Law 117-2, that awarded funds; and</p>
          <p>(4) "student support services personnel" means an individual licensed to serve as a school counselor, school psychologist, school social worker, school nurse, or chemical dependency counselor in Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.901.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of student support personnel aid is to:</p>
          <p>(1) address shortages of student support services personnel within Minnesota schools;</p>
          <p>(2) decrease caseloads for existing student support services personnel to ensure effective services;</p>
          <p>(3) ensure that students receive effective student support services and integrated and comprehensive services to improve prekindergarten through grade 12 academic, physical, social, and emotional outcomes supporting career and college readiness and effective school mental health services;</p>
          <p>(4) ensure that student support services personnel serve within the scope and practice of their training and licensure;</p>
          <p>(5) fully integrate learning supports, instruction, assessment, data-based decision making, and family and community engagement within a comprehensive approach that facilitates interdisciplinary collaboration; and</p>
          <p>(6) improve student health, school safety, and school climate to support academic success and career and college readiness.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.901.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Student support personnel aid.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The initial student support personnel aid for a school district equals the greater of the student support personnel allowance times the adjusted pupil units at the district for the current fiscal year or $40,000. The initial student support personnel aid for a charter school equals the greater of the student support personnel allowance times the adjusted pupil units at the charter school for the current fiscal year or $20,000. Aid under this paragraph must be reserved in a fund balance that, beginning in fiscal year 2025, may not exceed the greater of the aid entitlement in the prior fiscal year or the fund balance in the prior fiscal year.</p>
          <p>(b) The cooperative student support personnel aid for a school district that is a member of an intermediate school district or other cooperative unit that serves students equals the greater of the cooperative student support allowance times the adjusted pupil units at the district for the current fiscal year or $40,000. If a district is a member of more than one cooperative unit that serves students, the revenue must be allocated among the cooperative units. Aid under this paragraph must not exceed actual expenditures.</p>
          <p>(c) The student support personnel allowance equals $11.94 for fiscal year 2024, $17.08 for fiscal year 2025, and $48.73 for fiscal year 2026 and later.</p>
          <p>(d) The cooperative student support allowance equals $0.60 for fiscal year 2024, $0.85 for fiscal year 2025, and $2.44 for fiscal year 2026 and later.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.901.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Allowed uses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Aid under this section must be used to hire new positions for student support services personnel or increase a current position that is less than 1.0 full-time equivalent to a greater number of service hours or make permanent a position hired using onetime resources awarded through the federal Coronavirus Aid Relief and Economic Security Act, the federal Consolidated Appropriations Act, the federal Division M-Coronavirus Response and Relief Supplemental Appropriations Act, or the federal American Rescue Plan Act, or to maintain a position that would otherwise be eliminated.</p>
          <p>(b) Cooperative student support personnel aid must be transferred to the intermediate district or other cooperative unit of which the district is a member and used to hire new positions for student support services personnel or increase a current position that is less than 1.0 full-time equivalent to a greater number of service hours or make permanent a position hired using onetime resources awarded through the American Rescue Plan Act at the intermediate district or cooperative unit.</p>
          <p>(c) If a school district, charter school, or cooperative unit does not receive at least two applications and is not able to hire a new full-time equivalent position with student support personnel aid, the aid may be used for contracted services from individuals licensed to serve as a school counselor, school psychologist, school social worker, school nurse, or chemical dependency counselor in Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.901.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Report required.</span></h2>
          <p>By February 1 following any fiscal year in which student support personnel aid was received, a school district, charter school, or cooperative unit must submit a written report to the commissioner indicating how the new position affected two or more of the following measures:</p>
          <p>(1) school climate;</p>
          <p>(2) student health;</p>
          <p>(3) attendance rates;</p>
          <p>(4) academic achievement;</p>
          <p>(5) career and college readiness; and</p>
          <p>(6) postsecondary completion rates.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.901.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.5.63.0">2023 c 55 art 5 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.5.10.0">2024 c 115 art 5 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.91"><b>124D.91</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/294/#laws.5.22.0">2004 c 294 art 5 s 22</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.92"><b>124D.92</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/294/#laws.5.22.0">2004 c 294 art 5 s 22</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.93"><b>124D.93</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.8.8.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 8 s 8</a>]</div>

        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.95.1">

          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/133/#laws.2.21.0">2003 c 133 art 2 s 21</a>]</p>

          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/133/#laws.2.21.0">2003 c 133 art 2 s 21</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.95.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/133/#laws.2.21.0">2003 c 133 art 2 s 21</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.95.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/133/#laws.2.21.0">2003 c 133 art 2 s 21</a>]</p>

      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.955">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.955 HEALTHY KIDS AWARDS PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.955.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The healthy kids awards program is established to reward kindergarten through grade 12 schools that implement policies and practices that create opportunities for students to be physically active and make healthy food choices throughout the day. The program seeks to integrate physical activity into nonphysical education classes, recess, and extracurricular activities throughout the day in addition to the physical education received in physical education classes. The program also seeks to integrate nutrition education and healthy food and beverage choices throughout the school environment, including classrooms, cafeteria, vending, school stores, and fundraising. The program requirements align with the Institute of Medicine's guidelines for school food and beverages.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.955.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Participation by school districts.</span></h2>
          <p>School districts may submit letters of intent to participate in the healthy kids awards program to the commissioner of education by September 15 of each school year. Schools that report to the commissioner of education and meet the program criteria developed by the commissioner will have a "Healthy Kids Award" indicator placed on the school report card.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.955.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/396/#laws.0.8.0">2010 c 396 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.957">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.957 MINNESOTA YOUTH COUNCIL COMMITTEE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.957.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment and membership.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Youth Council Committee is established within and under the auspices of the Minnesota Alliance With Youth. The committee consists of four members from each congressional district in Minnesota and four members selected at-large. Members must be selected through an application and interview process conducted by the Minnesota Alliance With Youth. In making its appointments, the Minnesota Alliance With Youth should strive to ensure gender and ethnic diversity in the committee's membership. Members must be in grades 8 through 12 and serve two-year terms, except that one-half of the initial members must serve a one-year term. Members may serve a maximum of two terms.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.957.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Youth Council Committee shall:</p>
          <p>(1) provide advice and recommendations to the legislature and the governor on issues affecting youth;</p>
          <p>(2) serve as a liaison for youth around the state to the legislature and the governor; and</p>
          <p>(3) submit an annual report of the council's activities and goals.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.957.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Partnerships.</span></h2>
          <p>The Minnesota Youth Council Committee shall partner with nonprofits, the private sector, and educational resources to fulfill its duties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.957.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Youth Council Committee in the legislature.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The Minnesota Youth Council Committee shall meet at least twice each year during the regular session of the legislature.</p>
          <p>(b) The committee may:</p>
          <p>(1) select introduced bills in the house of representatives and senate for consideration for a public hearing before the committee;</p>
          <p>(2) propose youth legislation;</p>
          <p>(3) provide advisory opinions to the legislature on bills heard before the committee; and</p>
          <p>(4) prepare a youth omnibus bill.</p>
          <p>(c) The leaders of the majority and minority parties of the house of representatives and senate shall each appoint one legislator to serve as a legislative liaison to the committee. Leadership of the house of representatives and senate, on rotating years, may appoint a staff member to staff the committee.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.957.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/116/#laws.8.1.0">2013 c 116 art 8 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.2.10.0">2024 c 115 art 2 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.96">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.96 WELFARE AND CORRECTIONAL INSTITUTIONS' POLICIES FOR EDUCATIONAL PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <p>Before July 1 of each year, each welfare and correctional institution which offers an elementary, secondary or vocational educational program shall develop a written policy for its educational program for the next school year. The institutional policy shall specify the educational goals for the institution; instructional plans for implementing these goals; estimated number and grade level of students; number of licensed educational staff; areas of licensure; student to staff ratios; number of supervisory personnel; proposed educational budget; procedures for evaluation of the program; and any other information deemed necessary by the commissioner of education for the evaluation of the educational institutions. The institutions shall submit the policy to the commissioner of education who will review the policy to determine whether the program and personnel employed in the program are adequate to meet the institution's obligation to provide instruction and services in compliance with the Department of Education rules and standards. If necessary, the commissioner shall make recommendations to the institution for changes in its educational program.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.96.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/470/#laws.0.2.0">1982 c 470 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.124D.97"><b>124D.97</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/256/#laws.1.8.0">2004 c 256 art 1 s 8</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.98">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.98 LITERACY INCENTIVE AID.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.98.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Literacy incentive aid.</span></h2>
          <p> A district's literacy incentive aid equals the sum of the proficiency aid under subdivision 2, and the growth aid under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.98.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Proficiency aid.</span></h2>
          <p> The proficiency aid for each school in a district that has submitted to the commissioner its local literacy plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.12#stat.120B.12.4a">120B.12, subdivision 4a</a>, is equal to the product of the school's proficiency allowance times the number of third grade pupils at the school on October 1 of the previous fiscal year. A school's proficiency allowance is equal to the percentage of students in each building that meet or exceed proficiency on the third grade reading Minnesota Comprehensive Assessment, averaged across the previous three test administrations, times $530.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.98.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Growth aid.</span></h2>
          <p> The growth aid for each school in a district that has submitted to the commissioner its local literacy plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.12#stat.120B.12.4a">120B.12, subdivision 4a</a>, is equal to the product of the school's growth allowance times the number of fourth grade pupils enrolled at the school on October 1 of the previous fiscal year. A school's growth allowance is equal to the percentage of students at that school making medium or high growth, under subdivision 4, on the fourth grade reading Minnesota Comprehensive Assessment, averaged across the previous three test administrations, times $530.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.98.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Medium and high growth.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(b) "Medium growth" is an assessment score within one-half standard deviation above or below the average year-two assessment scores for students with similar year-one assessment scores.</p>
          <p>(c) "High growth" is an assessment score one-half standard deviation or more above the average year-two assessment scores for students with similar year-one assessment scores.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.98.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Literacy incentive aid uses.</span></h2>
          <p>A school district must use its literacy incentive aid to support implementation of evidence-based reading instruction. The following are eligible uses of literacy incentive aid:</p>
          <p>(1) training for kindergarten through grade 3 teachers, early childhood educators, special education teachers, reading intervention teachers working with students in kindergarten through grade 12, curriculum directors, and instructional support staff that provide reading instruction, on using evidence-based screening and progress monitoring tools;</p>
          <p>(2) evidence-based training using a training program approved by the Department of Education under the Read Act;</p>
          <p>(3) employing or contracting with a literacy lead, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.119">120B.119</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) employing an intervention specialist;</p>
          <p>(5) approved screeners, materials, training, and ongoing coaching to ensure reading interventions under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.56#stat.125A.56.1">125A.56, subdivision 1</a>, are evidence-based;</p>
          <p>(6) costs of substitute teachers to allow teachers to complete required training during the teachers' contract day; and</p>
          <p>(7) stipends for teachers completing training required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.12">120B.12</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.98.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/11/#laws.2.43.0">1Sp2011 c 11 art 2 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/239/#laws.2.16.0">2012 c 239 art 2 s 16</a>,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/1/3/#laws.2.59.0">1Sp2015 c 3 art 2 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.2.22.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 2 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.31.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.3.10.0">2023 c 55 art 3 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.3.3.0">2024 c 115 art 3 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.99">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.99 EDUCATION PARTNERSHIPS COALITION FUND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.99.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of education shall establish a program supporting a coalition of coordinated, aligned education partnerships as specified in this section, for a comprehensive network of evidence-based support services designed to close opportunity gaps by improving educational and developmental outcomes of children and their families within communities experiencing poverty and impediments to economic viability.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.99.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the terms defined in this subdivision have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(b) "Neighborhood partnership grant" means a sustaining grant for the ongoing operation, stability, and expansion of existing education partnership program locations serving a defined geographic area within a single municipality.</p>
          <p>(c) "Regional neighborhood partnership grant" means an implementation grant for the ongoing operation, stability, and expansion of activity of existing education partnership program locations serving a defined geographic area encompassing an entire municipality or part of or all of multiple municipalities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.99.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Administration; design.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish program requirements, an application process and timeline for each grant specified in subdivision 4, criteria for evaluation of applications, and a grant awards process. The commissioner's process must minimize administrative costs, minimize burdens for applicants and grant recipients, and provide a framework that permits flexibility in program design and implementation among grant recipients.</p>
          <p>(b) To the extent practicable, the commissioner shall design the program to align with programs implemented or proposed by organizations in Minnesota that:</p>
          <p>(1) identify and increase the capacity of organizations that are focused on achieving data-driven, locally controlled positive outcomes for children and youth throughout an entire neighborhood or geographic area through programs such as Strive Together, Promise Neighborhood, and the Education Partnerships Coalition members;</p>
          <p>(2) build a continuum of educational family and community supports with academically rigorous schools at the center;</p>
          <p>(3) maximize program efficiencies by integrating programmatic activities and eliminating administrative barriers;</p>
          <p>(4) develop local infrastructure needed to sustain and scale proven and effective solutions beyond the initial neighborhood or geographic area;</p>
          <p>(5) utilize appropriate outcome measures based on unique community needs and interests and apply rigorous evaluation on a periodic basis to be used to both monitor outcomes and allow for continuous improvements to systems;</p>
          <p>(6) collect and utilize data to improve student outcomes;</p>
          <p>(7) share disaggregated performance data with the community to set community-level outcomes;</p>
          <p>(8) employ continuous improvement processes;</p>
          <p>(9) have a Tribal entity, community foundation, higher education institution, or community-based organization as an anchor entity managing the partnership;</p>
          <p>(10) convene a cross-sector leadership group and have a documented accountability structure; and</p>
          <p>(11) demonstrate use of nonstate funds, from multiple sources, including in-kind contributions.</p>
          <p>(c) A grant recipient's supportive services programming must address:</p>
          <p>(1) kindergarten readiness and youth development;</p>
          <p>(2) grade 3 reading proficiency;</p>
          <p>(3) middle school mathematics;</p>
          <p>(4) high school graduation;</p>
          <p>(5) postsecondary educational enrollment;</p>
          <p>(6) postsecondary education completion or attainment;</p>
          <p>(7) physical and mental health;</p>
          <p>(8) development of career skills and readiness;</p>
          <p>(9) parental engagement and development;</p>
          <p>(10) community engagement and programmatic alignment; and</p>
          <p>(11) reduction of remedial education.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner, in consultation with grant recipients, must:</p>
          <p>(1) develop and revise core indicators of progress toward outcomes specifying impacts identified under subdivision 4;</p>
          <p>(2) establish a reporting system for grant recipients to measure program outcomes using data sources and program goals; and</p>
          <p>(3) evaluate effectiveness based on the core indicators established by each partnership.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.99.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>A grant recipient's program in the planning, development, or implementation phase must include:</p>
          <p>(1) integrated supportive services programming, as specified in subdivision 3, paragraph (c), within a specific community or geographic area for all ages of children and youth and their families within that area, provided that services may be phased in to all ages over time; and</p>
          <p>(2) a system for evaluating goals and outcomes as provided under subdivision 3, paragraph (d).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.99.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall award grants to qualifying recipients that can demonstrate a nonstate source of funds, including in-kind contributions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.99.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Legislative report.</span></h2>
          <p>By December 15 of each odd-numbered year, the commissioner shall submit a report on the education partnership program to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees having jurisdiction over kindergarten through grade 12 education, early childhood education, economic development, and human services. At a minimum, the report must summarize grantee activities, identify grant recipients and awards, analyze program performance measures and outcomes, and make any recommendations for legislative changes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.99.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.9.1.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 9 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.1.31.0">2018 c 182 art 1 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.9.2.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 9 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.11.8.0">2023 c 55 art 11 s 8</a>-10</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.991">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.991 PUBLIC SCHOOL LIBRARIES AND MEDIA CENTERS.</h1>
        <p>A school district or charter school library or school library media center provides equitable and free access to students, teachers, and administrators.</p>
        <p>A school library or school library media center must have the following characteristics:</p>
        <p>(1) ensures every student has equitable access to resources and is able to locate, access, and use resources that are organized and cataloged;</p>
        <p>(2) has a collection development plan that includes but is not limited to materials selection and deselection, a challenged materials procedure, and an intellectual and academic freedom statement;</p>
        <p>(3) is housed in a central location that provides an environment for expanded learning and supports a variety of student interests;</p>
        <p>(4) has technology and Internet access; and</p>
        <p>(5) is served by a licensed school library media specialist or licensed school librarian.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.991.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.9.6.0">2023 c 55 art 9 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.992">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.992 SCHOOL LIBRARY AID.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.992.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">School library aid.</span></h2>
          <p>For fiscal year 2024 and later, school library aid for a district equals the greater of $16.11 times the district's adjusted pupil units for the school year or $40,000. For fiscal year 2024 and later, school library aid for a charter school equals the greater of $16.11 times the charter school's adjusted pupil units for the school year or $20,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.992.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">State school librarian.</span></h2>
          <p>In fiscal year 2026 and each fiscal year thereafter, the Department of Education may retain up to $130,000 of the amount appropriated for school library aid under this section for the costs of the state school librarian under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.151">127A.151</a>. The aid for each school district and charter school under subdivision 1 must be reduced proportionately. The reduction in aid under this subdivision must be applied to the current year aid payment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.992.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Uses of school library aid.</span></h2>
          <p>School library aid must be reserved and used for directly funding the costs of the following purposes within a library:</p>
          <p>(1) the salaries and benefits of a school library media specialist;</p>
          <p>(2) electronic, computer, and audiovisual equipment;</p>
          <p>(3) information technology infrastructure and digital tools;</p>
          <p>(4) electronic and material resources; or</p>
          <p>(5) furniture, equipment, or supplies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.992.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>By January 15, 2025, and annually thereafter, the commissioner of education must report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees with jurisdiction over kindergarten through grade 12 education on how school districts and charter schools used aid under this section in the previous fiscal year. In preparing the report, the commissioner may use information available from the uniform financial accounting and reporting system. The report must be filed in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.195">3.195</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.992.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.9.15.0">2023 c 55 art 9 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.9.6.0">2024 c 115 art 9 s 6</a>,7,10</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.124D.995">
        <h1 class="shn">124D.995 SCHOOL UNEMPLOYMENT AID.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.995.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Account established.</span></h2>
          <p>An account is established in the special revenue fund known as the school unemployment aid account.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.995.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Funds deposited in account.</span></h2>
          <p>Funds appropriated for school unemployment aid must be transferred to the school unemployment aid account in the special revenue fund.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.995.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Money appropriated.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Subject to the availability of funds, money in the account is annually appropriated to the commissioner of education to reimburse school districts; charter schools; intermediate school districts and cooperative units under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>; the Perpich Center for Arts Education; and the Minnesota State Academies for costs associated with providing unemployment benefits to school employees under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/268.085#stat.268.085.7">268.085, subdivision 7</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) The Perpich Center for Arts Education and the Minnesota State Academies may only apply to the commissioner for reimbursement of unemployment insurance amounts in excess of the amounts specifically identified in their annual agency appropriations.</p>
          <p>(c) If the amount in the account is insufficient, the commissioner must proportionately reduce the aid payment to each recipient. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.45#stat.127A.45.3">127A.45, subdivision 3</a>, aid payments must be paid 90 percent in the current year and ten percent in the following year on a schedule determined by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.995.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Administration and monitoring.</span></h2>
          <p>Up to $275,000 is annually appropriated from the account to the commissioner of education for costs associated with administering and monitoring the program under this section. This amount is in addition to any other amount specifically appropriated for this purpose.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.995.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">School reimbursement.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of education must reimburse school districts, charter schools, intermediate school districts and other cooperative units, the Perpich Center for Arts Education, and the Minnesota State Academies in the form and manner specified by the commissioner. The commissioner may establish procedures to ensure that any costs reimbursed under this section are excluded from other school revenue calculations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.124D.995.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Expiration.</span></h2>
          <p>This section expires on June 30, 2027, and any balance remaining in the account is canceled to the general fund.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.124D.995.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.1.9.0">2023 c 55 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.1.5.0">2024 c 115 art 1 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
    </div>
